ANSWERS — CASE 7
QUESTION: How do I apply a filter in the Stores sub-module of PMS?
JUDGING RULE: must ['spares', 'vessel', 'filter']; must_not ['not covered', "isn't covered", 'not documented', 'does not cover', 'no information']; expected 'PMS User Manual' p(49, 47). Source: PMS Office R2 p49 §1.1.8.2 says 'Refer to the Spares sub-sub-module for the filter process and apply the same steps' → §1.1.7.7 p47: open Spares (Inventory tab by default), select the Vessel, search for parts/components, apply filters such as Criticality, Rotation Item and Stock. Answer must name the Spares section as the source.
PER-RUN (current judges): E0 [False, True, True]  E1 [True, True, True]

[1] (Technical - PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — 1.1.8.2 HOW TO APPLY FILTER (p.49))
* Refer to the ‘Spares’ sub-sub-module for the filter process and apply the same steps.

(Cross-reference resolved: the steps for Stores › How To Apply Filter are the same as section 1.1.7.7 'How To Apply Filter' under Spares, page 47. They are:)
* Click on the 'Spares' sub – sub module, and the system will open the 'Inventory' tab by default. (Ref Figure 80)

* The user can select the Vessel, search for parts/components, and apply filters such as Criticality, Rotation Item, and Stock to refine the displayed records. (Ref Figure 80)

* Click on the 'Spares' sub – sub module, and the system will open the 'Inventory' tab by default. (Ref Figure 66)

* The user can use the search field and dropdown filters (Criticality, Rotation Item, or Stock) to refine the displayed records. (Ref Figure 66)

* Choose a department from the All Departments dropdown. (Ref Figure 76)

Figure 76

FULL ANSWER:
For the **Stores** sub-module, use the procedure for your PMS environment:

**Office PMS**
1. Open the **Spares** sub-sub-module; the **Inventory** tab opens by default.
2. Select the **Vessel**.
3. Search for parts or components.
4. Apply filters such as **Criticality**, **Rotation Item**, and **Stock**.
5. Optionally select:
   - **Location** to view spares by physical storage location.
   - **History** to view the complete transaction history.

These are the same steps as **Spares › How To Apply Filter**, section **1.1.7.7, page 47**.

**Ship PMS**
1. Open the **Spares** sub-sub-module; the **Inventory** tab opens by default.
2. Use the **Search** field.
3. Use the dropdown filters **Criticality**, **Rotation Item**, or **Stock**.
4. Optionally select:
   - **Location** to view spares by physical storage location.
   - **History** to view the complete transaction history.

These are the same steps as **Spares › How To Apply Filter**, section **1.1.7.5, page 40**.

**Source:** Office method — *Technical - PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026*, section **1.1.8.2**, p.49, cross-referenced to section **1.1.7.7**, p.47. Ship method — *Technical - PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific_R3_08.07.2026*, section **1.1.8.2**, p.43, cross-referenced to section **1.1.7.5**, p.40.

FULL ANSWER:
The Stores filter process is the same as the Spares filter process:

**Office PMS**
1. Open the **Spares** sub-sub-module; the **Inventory** tab opens by default.
2. Select the **Vessel**, search for parts/components, and apply **Criticality**, **Rotation Item**, or **Stock** filters.
3. Use the **Location** tab to view spares by physical storage location, or the **History** tab to view all spare transactions.

**Vessel PMS**
1. Open the **Spares** sub-sub-module; the **Inventory** tab opens by default.
2. Use the **Search** field and the **Criticality**, **Rotation Item**, or **Stock** dropdown filters to refine the records.
3. Use the **Location** tab for storage-location records, or the **History** tab for complete spare transaction history.

These Stores steps are the same as the Spares procedures: section 1.1.7.7, page 47 for Office PMS, and section 1.1.7.5, page 40 for Vessel PMS.

**Source:**  
- Office PMS: *PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026*, section 1.1.8.2, p.49; resolved to section 1.1.7.7, p.47.  
- Vessel PMS: *PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific_R3_08.07.2026*, section 1.1.8.2, p.43; resolved to section 1.1.7.5, p.40.

FULL ANSWER:
The excerpts support two Stores filtering procedures:

**Office PMS** — the Stores steps are the same as **Spares, section 1.1.7.7, page 47**:

1. Click **Spares** in the sub-sub-module menu; the **Inventory** tab opens by default.
2. Select the **Vessel**, search for parts or components, and use the **Criticality**, **Rotation Item**, and **Stock** filters to refine the records.
3. Use the **Location** tab to view spares by storage location, or the **History** tab to view all spare transactions.

**Vessel PMS** — the Stores steps are the same as **Spares, section 1.1.7.5, page 40**:

1. Click **Spares** in the sub-sub-module menu; the **Inventory** tab opens by default.
2. Use the search field and the **Criticality**, **Rotation Item**, or **Stock** dropdown filters to refine the displayed records.
3. Use the **Location** tab to view spares by storage location, or the **History** tab to view all spare transactions.

**Source:**
- Office PMS: *Technical - PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026*, section 1.1.8.2, page 49; resolved cross-reference to section 1.1.7.7, page 47.
- Vessel PMS: *Technical - PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific_R3_08.07.2026*, section 1.1.8.2, page 43; resolved cross-reference to section 1.1.7.5, page 40.

FULL ANSWER:
The Stores filter process is the same as the Spares “How to Apply Filter” process:

**Office PMS**
1. Open **Stores > Spares**. The **Inventory** tab opens by default.
2. Select the **Vessel**, search for parts/components, and apply **Criticality**, **Rotation Item**, or **Stock** filters to refine the records.
3. Use **Location** to view spares by physical storage location, or **History** to view all spare transactions.

**Ship PMS**
1. Open **Stores > Spares**. The **Inventory** tab opens by default.
2. Use the **Search** field and the **Criticality**, **Rotation Item**, or **Stock** dropdown filters to refine the records.
3. Use **Location** to view spares by physical storage location, or **History** to view all spare transactions.

The Stores procedure is explicitly resolved as the same as the Spares procedure: Office, section 1.1.7.7, page 47; Ship, section 1.1.7.5, page 40.

**Source:**  
- Office: *PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin*, section 1.1.8.2, p.49; same steps as section 1.1.7.7, p.47.  
- Ship: *PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific*, section 1.1.8.2, p.43; same steps as section 1.1.7.5, p.40.

FULL ANSWER:
The Stores filter process is documented separately for Office and Vessel PMS:

**Office PMS — Stores**
1. Open the **Spares** sub-sub-module; the **Inventory** tab opens by default.
2. Select the **Vessel**.
3. Search for parts or components.
4. Apply **Criticality**, **Rotation Item**, or **Stock** filters to refine the records.
5. Use the **Location** tab to view spares by storage location, or the **History** tab to view all spare transactions.

These steps are the same as **Spares › How To Apply Filter**, section 1.1.7.7, page 47.

**Vessel PMS — Stores**
1. Open the **Spares** sub-sub-module; the **Inventory** tab opens by default.
2. Use the **Search** field.
3. Apply the **Criticality**, **Rotation Item**, or **Stock** dropdown filters.
4. Use the **Location** tab for physical storage locations, or the **History** tab for complete spare transaction history.

These steps are the same as **Spares › How To Apply Filter**, section 1.1.7.5, page 40.

**Source:**  
- Office method: *PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin*, section 1.1.8.2, p.49; resolved to section 1.1.7.7, p.47.  
- Vessel method: *PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific*, section 1.1.8.2, p.43; resolved to section 1.1.7.5, p.40.

FULL ANSWER:
For the **Stores** sub-module, the procedure differs slightly between Office and Ship PMS:

**Office PMS** — same steps as **Spares › How To Apply Filter**, section 1.1.7.7, page 47:

1. Open the **Spares** sub-sub-module. The **Inventory** tab opens by default.
2. Select the **Vessel**.
3. Search for parts or components.
4. Apply filters such as **Criticality**, **Rotation Item**, and **Stock**.
5. Use the **Location** tab to view spares by physical storage location, or the **History** tab to view all spare transactions.

**Ship PMS** — same steps as **Spares › How To Apply Filter**, section 1.1.7.5, page 40:

1. Open the **Spares** sub-sub-module. The **Inventory** tab opens by default.
2. Use the search field to find parts or components.
3. Apply the **Criticality**, **Rotation Item**, or **Stock** dropdown filters.
4. Use the **Location** tab to view spares by physical storage location, or the **History** tab to view all spare transactions.

**Source:**
- Office PMS: Technical - PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026, section 1.1.8.2, p.49; resolved to section 1.1.7.7, p.47.
- Ship PMS: Technical - PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific_R3_08.07.2026, section 1.1.8.2, p.43; resolved to section 1.1.7.5, p.40.

GENERATED — CASE 13
QUESTION: On the ship, what happens if I enter a running-hours reading lower than the component's current hours when completing a work order?
JUDGING RULE: must ['refused', 'back-dated']; must_not ['not covered', "isn't covered", 'not documented', 'does not cover', 'no information', 'still saved and the reading is kept']; expected manual 'Ship-Side' pages None. Source: R3 §1.1.13.2: refused ('Running hours cannot go backward!'); back-dated exception (rhTimelineValidationService.ts:310-311; workOrderCompletionService.ts:257-265). R2 said still saved.
PER-RUN (current judges): E0 [True, True, True]  E1 [False, True, True]

==============================================================================================================
[E0] run 1 — verdict True — gate=answer cite=Technical - Ship-Side (Ves pNone | 'When completing a running-hours work order on the ship:\n\n1. A ru'
CITATIONS SHOWN TO THE USER:
  [1] Technical - Ship-Side (Vessel Crew) Operational Notes — 1.1.13.2 Completing Work Orders on the Ship — Current Rules
  [2] Technical - Recent Updates & Changed Behaviours (Operational) Notes — 1.1.14.2 Completing a Running-Hours Work Order Requires a Reading
  [3] Technical - Recent Updates & Changed Behaviours (Operational) Notes — 1.1.14.3 Running Hours Never Move Backwards
  [4] Technical - PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific_R3_08.07.2026 — 1.1.6.3 HOW TO UPDATE RUNNING HOURS. (p.33)
  [5] Technical - PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific_R3_08.07.2026 — TECHNICAL USER MANUAL (p.34)

CAPTURED MODEL INPUT (the excerpts exactly as sent):
[1] (Technical - Ship-Side (Vessel Crew) Operational Notes — 1.1.13.2 Completing Work Orders on the Ship — Current Rules)
* Running-hours jobs need a reading. When you complete a work order for a job based on running hours you must enter the running-hours reading at completion; the form does not accept the completion without it. Exception: components marked as not running-hours driven do not require a reading. The per-vessel 'RH validation' switch (Sail Admin) can relax the running-hours checks for a vessel.

* A reading lower than the component's current hours is refused ('Running hours cannot go backward!'). The one exception is a back-dated entry: if the reading date you enter is earlier than the date of the component's current reading, the completion is saved and the reading is kept on the work order for scheduling, but the component's counter is not changed.

* The completion reading cannot be higher than the component's current meter reading (the work cannot have been done at more hours than the meter shows now).

* Hours only legitimately go down for a meter replacement or a renewal reset, recorded as such.

* Save as Draft: you can save partially filled completion details ('Save Draft') and return later. A draft changes nothing on the live work order and does not change its status until you su

---

[2] (Technical - Recent Updates & Changed Behaviours (Operational) Notes — 1.1.14.2 Completing a Running-Hours Work Order Requires a Reading)
* When completing a work order for a job based on running hours, the running-hours reading at completion is mandatory — the completion is refused without it. Components marked as not running-hours driven are exempt.

* The reading is recorded on the work order together with the outcome of applying it (whether it updated the component's counter, or was kept on record without changing the counter — see the next item).

---

[3] (Technical - Recent Updates & Changed Behaviours (Operational) Notes — 1.1.14.3 Running Hours Never Move Backwards)
* A component's running-hours counter is never reduced by a late, low or out-of-order reading from any source (ship entry, office entry, work-order completion or sync). A lower reading entered directly is refused; a lower reading that is back-dated is kept in the audit history and on the work order without changing the counter.

* The only legitimate decreases are a meter replacement or a renewal reset, recorded as such when entered.

* Across sync, readings are reconciled by reading date — the latest date wins; a same-date tie goes to the ship. See the Sync (Operational) manual, section on running hours.

---

[4] (Technical - PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific_R3_08.07.2026 — 1.1.6.3 HOW TO UPDATE RUNNING HOURS. (p.33))
* Click on the 'Running Hrs' sub – sub module. (Ref Figure 52)

* Click on the '+ Bulk Update RH' button to update multiple components at once. (Ref Figure 52)

Figure 54

Figure 55

<mark>icon: hand pointing right For components with an Inherited RH type, updates are cascaded to all child components.</mark>

FULL ANSWER:
When completing a running-hours work order on the ship:

1. A running-hours reading is required, unless the component is marked as not running-hours driven.
2. If the reading is lower than the component’s current hours, it is refused with **“Running hours cannot go backward!”**
3. Exception: if the reading is **back-dated**—its date is earlier than the date of the component’s current reading—the completion is saved. The reading remains on the work order for scheduling, but the component’s running-hours counter is not changed.
4. The only legitimate decreases are those recorded as a **meter replacement** or **renewal reset**.
5. The vessel’s **RH validation** switch in Sail Admin can relax the running-hours checks.

**Source:** Ship-Side Operational Notes, §1.1.13.2 “Completing Work Orders on the Ship — Current Rules”; Recent Updates & Changed Behaviours Operational Notes, §§1.1.14.2–1.1.14.3.

A lower reading entered directly is refused; a lower reading that is back-dated is kept in the audit history and on the work order without changing the counter.

* The only legitimate decreases are a meter replacement or a renewal reset, recorded as such when entered.

* Across sync, readings are reconciled by reading date — the latest date wins; a same-date tie goes to the ship. See the Sync (Operational) manual, section on running hours.

FULL ANSWER:
1. If the completion reading is lower than the component’s current hours and is entered normally, completion is refused with **“Running hours cannot go backward!”** The component’s counter is not reduced.

2. If the reading is **back-dated**—its date is earlier than the date of the component’s current reading—the work order can be saved. The lower reading is retained on the work order and in the audit history for scheduling, but the component’s running-hours counter is not changed.

3. The only legitimate decreases are those recorded as a **meter replacement** or **renewal reset**.

**Source:**  
- Normal and back-dated work-order completion: *Technical - Ship-Side (Vessel Crew) Operational Notes*, §1.1.13.2, “Completing Work Orders on the Ship — Current Rules.”  
- Lower-reading handling: *Technical - Recent Updates & Changed Behaviours (Operational) Notes*, §§1.1.14.2–1.1.14.3.

CAPTURED MODEL INPUT (the excerpts exactly as sent):
[1] (Technical - Recent Updates & Changed Behaviours (Operational) Notes — 1.1.14.6 Office Generation of Work Orders Is a Per-Vessel Switch)
* Only a Sail Admin can generate work orders directly from the office, and only for a vessel whose 'office work-order generation' switch is enabled (off by default). The switch is on the 'Lead Time & Grace Period Settings' screen.

* With the switch off, the Sail Admin is told that office work-order generation is not enabled for the vessel and where to enable it. Other office roles are refused with a role message.

* Ships always generate their own work orders on their daily scan, regardless of this switch.

* The office 'Generate Now' button (Work Orders screen, with one vessel selected) runs an immediate, vessel-scoped generation instead of waiting for the daily sweep, subject to the same switch and role rule.

---

[2] (Technical - Recent Updates & Changed Behaviours (Operational) Notes — 1.1.14.7 Office Entry of Running Hours Is a Per-Vessel Switch)
* Office users can enter running hours for a vessel only if that vessel's 'office running-hours entry' switch is enabled (off by default). An office entry must carry an explicit reading date. Ship entry is unaffected.

---

[3] (Technical - KB pilot: Office 'Generate Now' — generate a vessel's due work orders from the office.md — Office 'Generate Now' — generate a vessel's due work orders from the office)
Provenance note: draft code-derived guidance, not a published manual. 3 statement(s) come from the June PMS manuals; 10 statement(s) about roles, switches and automatic generation were inspected in the Technical application code at repository revision cf5241ad6, which the application owner has confirmed is the code running in the Technical application. Where this guidance and a manual differ, say which source says what.
Applies to: Office
Who can do it (role): Sail Admin only. Any other office role is refused by the server with "Only a Sail Admin may generate work orders directly from the office."
Prerequisites (switches, settings, job/component state):
- The selected vessel's 'office work-order generation' switch must be ON. It is OFF by default for every vessel; a Sail Admin enables it per vessel on the 'Lead Time & Grace Period Settings' screen. With the switch off the server refuses with "Office work-order generation is not enabled for this vessel."
- One specific vessel must be selected on the Work Orders screen — the button is disabled for 'All' or 'My' vessels.
- Only jobs that are due and have no active work order are generated — the same rules as the ship's scan.
Steps:
1. Open the 'Work Orders' sub-sub-module and select the vessel from the 'Vessel' dropdown.
2. Click 'Generate Now' in the toolbar of the Work Orders screen (next to 'Planner' and 'Export').
3. The server runs the generation sweep for that vessel only and returns; the new work orders appear under the Scheduled / Due / Overdue tabs.
Exceptions / edge cases:
- The button is shown to Sail Admin, Client Admin and Head of Dept on the office instance, but the server accepts Sail Admin only; the other two roles see a button whose every click is refused. Recorded in CONFLICTS.md (existing standalone note).
- On a ship instance this gate always allows generation (the ship's own scan is the normal path).
- 'Generate Now' does NOT apply to the per-job 'Generate WO' button on the Components page, which has its own, different condition — see office-generate-wo-per-job.md.
Where the manual differs:
- The June manuals do not mention 'Generate Now', the Sail Admin restriction or the per-vessel switch. Recorded in CONFLICTS.md.
Related procedures: planned-wo-ship-daily-scan.md · office-generate-wo-per-job.md · unplanned-wo.md

---

[4] (Technical - Roles & Permissions (Operational) User Manual — 1.1.12.9 Per-Vessel Administrative Switches (Sail Admin))
* Some behaviours are switched per vessel by a Sail Admin on the 'Lead Time & Grace Period Settings' screen:

* Office work-order generation — whether a Sail Admin may generate this vessel's planned work orders from the office (off by default; ships always generate their own regardless).

* Office running-hours entry — whether office users may enter running hours for this vessel (off by default; an explicit reading date is required).

* RH validation — whether the running-hours checks are applied for this vessel (on by default).

* Superintendent lock — restricts certain vessel actions to the superintendent when enabled.

* If an action is unavailable for one vessel but works for another, check these per-vessel switches before suspecting a permissions problem.

---

[5] (Technical - PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — 1.1.6.1 HOW TO ACCESS RUNNING HOURS (p.38))
* Click the ‘Running Hrs’ sub-sub-module; the default landing page is ‘Overview’. (Ref Figure 61)
* Select the vessel from the ‘Vessel’ dropdown to view the current running hour details. (Ref Figure 61)

Screenshot showing the Running Hours page with callouts for clicking Running Hrs and selecting a vessel

Figure 61

FULL ANSWER:
The per-vessel switches are held on the **Lead Time & Grace Period Settings** screen.

This includes:

1. **Office work-order generation** — off by default; enables a Sail Admin to generate planned work orders for that vessel from the office. Ships generate their own work orders regardless of this switch.
2. **Office running-hours entry** — off by default; enables office users to enter running hours for that vessel. An office entry requires an explicit reading date.

**Source:**
- Technical — *Roles & Permissions (Operational) User Manual*, §1.1.12.9, “Per-Vessel Administrative Switches (Sail Admin)”
- Technical — *Recent Updates & Changed Behaviours (Operational) Notes*, §§1.1.14.6–1.1.14.7

[1] (Technical - Recent Updates & Changed Behaviours (Operational) Notes — 1.1.14.6 Office Generation of Work Orders Is a Per-Vessel Switch)
* Only a Sail Admin can generate work orders directly from the office, and only for a vessel whose 'office work-order generation' switch is enabled (off by default). The switch is on the 'Lead Time & Grace Period Settings' screen.

* With the switch off, the Sail Admin is told that office work-order generation is not enabled for the vessel and where to enable it. Other office roles are refused with a role message.

* Ships always generate their own work orders on their daily scan, regardless of this switch.

* The office 'Generate Now' button (Work Orders screen, with one vessel selected) runs an immediate, vessel-scoped generation instead of waiting for the daily sweep, subject to the same switch and role rule.

---

[2] (Technical - Recent Updates & Changed Behaviours (Operational) Notes — 1.1.14.7 Office Entry of Running Hours Is a Per-Vessel Switch)
* Office users can enter running hours for a vessel only if that vessel's 'office running-hours entry' switch is enabled (off by default). An office entry must carry an explicit reading date. Ship entry is unaffected.

---

[3] (Technical - KB pilot: Office 'Generate Now' — generate a vessel's due work orders from the office.md — Office 'Generate Now' — generate a vessel's due work orders from the office)
Provenance note: draft code-derived guidance, not a published manual. 3 statement(s) come from the June PMS manuals; 10 statement(s) about roles, switches and automatic generation were inspected in the Technical application code at repository revision cf5241ad6, which the application owner has confirmed is the code running in the Technical application. Where this guidance and a manual differ, say which source says what.
Applies to: Office
Who can do it (role): Sail Admin only. Any other office role is refused by the server with "Only a Sail Admin may generate work orders directly from the office."
Prerequisites (switches, settings, job/component state):
- The selected vessel's 'office work-order generation' switch must be ON. It is OFF by default for every vessel; a Sail Admin enables it per vessel on the 'Lead Time & Grace Period Settings' screen. With the switch off the server refuses with "Office work-order generation is not enabled for this vessel."
- One specific vessel must be selected on the Work Orders screen — the button is disabled for 'All' or 'My' vessels.
- Only jobs that are due and have no active work order are generated — the same rules as the ship's scan.
Steps:
1. Open the 'Work Orders' sub-sub-module and select the vessel from the 'Vessel' dropdown.
2. Click 'Generate Now' in the toolbar of the Work Orders screen (next to 'Planner' and 'Export').
3. The server runs the generation sweep for that vessel only and returns; the new work orders appear under the Scheduled / Due / Overdue tabs.
Exceptions / edge cases:
- The button is shown to Sail Admin, Client Admin and Head of Dept on the office instance, but the server accepts Sail Admin only; the other two roles see a button whose every click is refused. Recorded in CONFLICTS.md (existing standalone note).
- On a ship instance this gate always allows generation (the ship's own scan is the normal path).
- 'Generate Now' does NOT apply to the per-job 'Generate WO' button on the Components page, which has its own, different condition — see office-generate-wo-per-job.md.
Where the manual differs:
- The June manuals do not mention 'Generate Now', the Sail Admin restriction or the per-vessel switch. Recorded in CONFLICTS.md.
Related procedures: planned-wo-ship-daily-scan.md · office-generate-wo-per-job.md · unplanned-wo.md

---

[4] (Technical - Roles & Permissions (Operational) User Manual — 1.1.12.9 Per-Vessel Administrative Switches (Sail Admin))
* Some behaviours are switched per vessel by a Sail Admin on the 'Lead Time & Grace Period Settings' screen:

* Office work-order generation — whether a Sail Admin may generate this vessel's planned work orders from the office (off by default; ships always generate their own regardless).

* Office running-hours entry — whether office users may enter running hours for this vessel (off by default; an explicit reading date is required).

* RH validation — whether the running-hours checks are applied for this vessel (on by default).

* Superintendent lock — restricts certain vessel actions to the superintendent when enabled.

* If an action is unavailable for one vessel but works for another, check these per-vessel switches before suspecting a permissions problem.

---

[5] (Technical - PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — 1.1.6.1 HOW TO ACCESS RUNNING HOURS (p.38))
* Click the ‘Running Hrs’ sub-sub-module; the default landing page is ‘Overview’. (Ref Figure 61)
* Select the vessel from the ‘Vessel’ dropdown to view the current running hour details. (Ref Figure 61)

Screenshot showing the Running Hours page with callouts for clicking Running Hrs and selecting a vessel

Figure 61

FULL ANSWER:
The per-vessel switches are on the **Lead Time & Grace Period Settings** screen. A **Sail Admin** manages them there, including:

- **Office work-order generation** — off by default.
- **Office running-hours entry** — off by default.

**Source:**  
- Technical — *Roles & Permissions (Operational) User Manual*, §1.1.12.9, “Per-Vessel Administrative Switches (Sail Admin)”  
- Technical — *Recent Updates & Changed Behaviours (Operational) Notes*, §§1.1.14.6–1.1.14.7

[3] (Technical - Roles & Permissions (Operational) User Manual — 1.1.12.9 Per-Vessel Administrative Switches (Sail Admin))
* Some behaviours are switched per vessel by a Sail Admin on the 'Lead Time & Grace Period Settings' screen:

* Office work-order generation — whether a Sail Admin may generate this vessel's planned work orders from the office (off by default; ships always generate their own regardless).

* Office running-hours entry — whether office users may enter running hours for this vessel (off by default; an explicit reading date is required).

* RH validation — whether the running-hours checks are applied for this vessel (on by default).

* Superintendent lock — restricts certain vessel actions to the superintendent when enabled.

* If an action is unavailable for one vessel but works for another, check these per-vessel switches before suspecting a permissions problem.

---

[4] (Technical - Recent Updates & Changed Behaviours (Operational) Notes — 1.1.14.6 Office Generation of Work Orders — Conditions by Action)
Generation, codes ROLE_NOT_PERMITTED, OFFICE_GENERATION_DISABLED, VESSEL_STATE_UNKNOWN]

* Office per-job 'Generate WO' (Components → the component's job, choose a reason) — the vessel's 'office work-order generation' switch must be enabled, and the job must exist, be active and not already have an active work order; the reason must be Planning, Breakdown or Other. There is NO action-specific role check on this path: it does not go through the role gate. Any signed-in user with access to the vessel may use it while the switch is on. [code: jobService.generateWorkOrder → isOfficeWoGenerationEnabled only; jobDueScanner.generateWorkOrderForJob for the active-work-order check]

* Unplanned work order ('+ Unplanned W\.O' on the Work Orders screen) — neither the Sail Admin rule nor the office generation switch applies to it. Normal sign-in and vessel access still apply. [code: the create path does not call the generation gate]

* Ships always generate their own work orders on their daily scan, regardless of this switch; a ship instance is allowed through the gate without the role or switch test. [code: evaluateDirectGeneration returns allowed for isShip]

* Applies to all of the above: n

---

[5] (Technical - Recent Updates & Changed Behaviours (Operational) Notes — 1.1.14.6 Office Generation of Work Orders — Conditions by Action)
* Office 'Generate Now' (Work Orders screen, one vessel selected) — runs an immediate, vessel-scoped generation instead of waiting for the daily sweep. Two conditions apply, both on the server: the caller's role must be Sail Admin, and that vessel's 'office work-order generation' switch must be enabled. The switch is off by default and is set on the 'Lead Time & Grace Period Settings' screen. [code: workOrderController.generateNow → workOrderGenerationGate.evaluateDirectGeneration; PROVISIONING_ROLE = 'Sail Admin'; isOfficeWoGenerationEnabled]

* Refusals for 'Generate Now' are distinct. A caller who is not a Sail Admin is refused with: "Only a Sail Admin may generate work orders directly from the office." A Sail Admin acting on a vessel whose switch is off is refused with: "Office work-order generation is not enabled for this vessel. A Sail Admin can enable it per vessel on the Lead Time & Grace Period Settings screen." A vessel whose provisioning state cannot be read is refused as well — the check fails closed. [code: evaluateDirectGeneration, codes ROLE_NOT_PERMITTED, OFFICE_GENERATION_DISABLED, VESSEL_STATE_UNKNOWN]

* Office per-job 'Generate WO' (Components → the component's

FULL ANSWER:
The per-vessel switches are held on the **Lead Time & Grace Period Settings** screen. A **Sail Admin** configures switches such as:

- Office work-order generation
- Office running-hours entry
- RH validation
- Superintendent lock

**Source:** Technical — Roles & Permissions (Operational) User Manual, §1.1.12.9; Technical — Recent Updates & Changed Behaviours (Operational) Notes, §§1.1.14.6–1.1.14.7.

- 'Generate Now' does NOT apply to the per-job 'Generate WO' button on the Components page, which has its own, different condition — see office-generate-wo-per-job.md.
Where the manual differs:
- The June manuals do not mention 'Generate Now', the Sail Admin restriction or the per-vessel switch. Recorded in CONFLICTS.md.
Related procedures: planned-wo-ship-daily-scan.md · office-generate-wo-per-job.md · unplanned-wo.md

[3] (Technical - Roles & Permissions (Operational) User Manual — 1.1.12.9 Per-Vessel Administrative Switches (Sail Admin))
* Some behaviours are switched per vessel by a Sail Admin on the 'Lead Time & Grace Period Settings' screen:

* Office work-order generation — whether a Sail Admin may generate this vessel's planned work orders from the office (off by default; ships always generate their own regardless).

* Office running-hours entry — whether office users may enter running hours for this vessel (off by default; an explicit reading date is required).

* RH validation — whether the running-hours checks are applied for this vessel (on by default).

* Superintendent lock — restricts certain vessel actions to the superintendent when enabled.

* If an action is unavailable for one vessel but works for another, check these per-vessel switches before suspecting a permissions problem.

---

[4] (Technical - Recent Updates & Changed Behaviours (Operational) Notes — 1.1.14.6 Office Generation of Work Orders — Conditions by Action)
Generation, codes ROLE_NOT_PERMITTED, OFFICE_GENERATION_DISABLED, VESSEL_STATE_UNKNOWN]

* Office per-job 'Generate WO' (Components → the component's job, choose a reason) — the vessel's 'office work-order generation' switch must be enabled, and the job must exist, be active and not already have an active work order; the reason must be Planning, Breakdown or Other. There is NO action-specific role check on this path: it does not go through the role gate. Any signed-in user with access to the vessel may use it while the switch is on. [code: jobService.generateWorkOrder → isOfficeWoGenerationEnabled only; jobDueScanner.generateWorkOrderForJob for the active-work-order check]

* Unplanned work order ('+ Unplanned W\.O' on the Work Orders screen) — neither the Sail Admin rule nor the office generation switch applies to it. Normal sign-in and vessel access still apply. [code: the create path does not call the generation gate]

* Ships always generate their own work orders on their daily scan, regardless of this switch; a ship instance is allowed through the gate without the role or switch test. [code: evaluateDirectGeneration returns allowed for isShip]

[5] (Technical - Recent Updates & Changed Behaviours (Operational) Notes — 1.1.14.6 Office Generation of Work Orders — Conditions by Action)
* Office 'Generate Now' (Work Orders screen, one vessel selected) — runs an immediate, vessel-scoped generation instead of waiting for the daily sweep. Two conditions apply, both on the server: the caller's role must be Sail Admin, and that vessel's 'office work-order generation' switch must be enabled. The switch is off by default and is set on the 'Lead Time & Grace Period Settings' screen. [code: workOrderController.generateNow → workOrderGenerationGate.evaluateDirectGeneration; PROVISIONING_ROLE = 'Sail Admin'; isOfficeWoGenerationEnabled]

* Refusals for 'Generate Now' are distinct. A caller who is not a Sail Admin is refused with: "Only a Sail Admin may generate work orders directly from the office." A Sail Admin acting on a vessel whose switch is off is refused with: "Office work-order generation is not enabled for this vessel. A Sail Admin can enable it per vessel on the Lead Time & Grace Period Settings screen." A vessel whose provisioning state cannot be read is refused as well — the check fails closed. [code: evaluateDirectGeneration, codes ROLE_NOT_PERMITTED, OFFICE_GENERATION_DISABLED, VESSEL_STATE_UNKNOWN]

* Office per-job 'Generate WO' (Components → the component's

FULL ANSWER:
The per-vessel switches are held on the **Lead Time & Grace Period Settings** screen. A **Sail Admin** manages switches such as:

- Office work-order generation
- Office running-hours entry
- RH validation
- Superintendent lock

Source: Roles & Permissions (Operational) User Manual, §1.1.12.9; Recent Updates & Changed Behaviours (Operational) Notes, §§1.1.14.6–1.1.14.7.

[2] (Technical - KB pilot: Office 'Generate Now' — generate a vessel's due work orders from the office.md — Office 'Generate Now' — generate a vessel's due work orders from the office)
Provenance note: draft code-derived guidance, not a published manual. 3 statement(s) come from the June PMS manuals; 10 statement(s) about roles, switches and automatic generation were inspected in the Technical application code at repository revision cf5241ad6, which the application owner has confirmed is the code running in the Technical application. Where this guidance and a manual differ, say which source says what.
Applies to: Office
Who can do it (role): Sail Admin only. Any other office role is refused by the server with "Only a Sail Admin may generate work orders directly from the office."
Prerequisites (switches, settings, job/component state):
- The selected vessel's 'office work-order generation' switch must be ON. It is OFF by default for every vessel; a Sail Admin enables it per vessel on the 'Lead Time & Grace Period Settings' screen. With the switch off the server refuses with "Office work-order generation is not enabled for this vessel."
- One specific vessel must be selected on the Work Orders screen — the button is disabled for 'All' or 'My' vessels.
- Only jobs that are due and have no active work order are generated — the same rules as the ship's scan.
Steps:
1. Open the 'Work Orders' sub-sub-module and select the vessel from the 'Vessel' dropdown.
2. Click 'Generate Now' in the toolbar of the Work Orders screen (next to 'Planner' and 'Export').
3. The server runs the generation sweep for that vessel only and returns; the new work orders appear under the Scheduled / Due / Overdue tabs.
Exceptions / edge cases:
- The button is shown to Sail Admin, Client Admin and Head of Dept on the office instance, but the server accepts Sail Admin only; the other two roles see a button whose every click is refused. Recorded in CONFLICTS.md (existing standalone note).
- On a ship instance this gate always allows generation (the ship's own scan is the normal path).
- 'Generate Now' does NOT apply to the per-job 'Generate WO' button on the Components page, which has its own, different condition — see office-generate-wo-per-job.md.
Where the manual differs:
- The June manuals do not mention 'Generate Now', the Sail Admin restriction or the per-vessel switch. Recorded in CONFLICTS.md.
Related procedures: planned-wo-ship-daily-scan.md · office-generate-wo-per-job.md · unplanned-wo.md

---

[3] (Technical - KB pilot: How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order.md — How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order)
Provenance note: draft code-derived guidance, not a published manual. 3 statement(s) come from the June PMS manuals; 7 statement(s) about roles, switches and automatic generation were inspected in the Technical application code at repository revision cf5241ad6, which the application owner has confirmed is the code running in the Technical application. Where this guidance and a manual differ, say which source says what.
Applies to: Both
Who can do it (role): Depends on the way — (1) planned: nobody, the ship system creates them; office 'Generate Now': Sail Admin only; (2) per-job 'Generate WO': no role check; (3) unplanned: no role check. Normal sign-in and vessel access apply to all.
Prerequisites (switches, settings, job/component state): Office 'Generate Now' and office per-job 'Generate WO' both need the vessel's 'office work-order generation' switch ON (off by default, set by a Sail Admin on 'Lead Time & Grace Period Settings'). Unplanned needs no switch. A job with an active work order cannot get another one.
Steps:
1. Planned (scheduled) work orders — generated by the system from each job's frequency and next due date. ON THE SHIP: automatic, once a day, no user action. IN THE OFFICE: nothing runs automatically; a Sail Admin can click 'Generate Now' on the Work Orders screen for one vessel, only if that vessel's switch is on. Details: planned-wo-ship-daily-scan.md and office-generate-now.md.
2. One job on demand — Components → (Office: select the vessel) → open the component → Part C → 'Generate WO' → choose the reason (Planning / Breakdown / Other). No role check on this path (sign-in and vessel access still apply); in the office only if the vessel switch is on; the job must be active and must not already have an active work order. Details: office-generate-wo-per-job.md.
3. Unplanned work — Work Orders → (Office: select the vessel) → '+ Unplanned W.O' → Part A, Save → Part B, Submit Work Order. No role check on this path, no switch. Details: unplanned-wo.md.
Exceptions / edge cases: The Sail Admin requirement belongs to 'Generate Now' only; it does not apply to per-job 'Generate WO' or to unplanned work orders.
Where the manual differs: The June manuals describe only ways 2 and 3 and state no office conditions; automatic ship generation and 'Generate Now' are absent from them. See CONFLICTS.md.
Related procedures: planned-wo-ship-daily-scan.md · office-generate-now.md · office-generate-wo-per-job.md · unplanned-wo.md

---

[4] (Technical - KB pilot: Planned work orders — generated automatically by the ship's daily job-due scan.md — Planned work orders — generated automatically by the ship's daily job-due scan)
Provenance note: draft code-derived guidance, not a published manual. 6 statement(s) come from the June PMS manuals; 7 statement(s) about roles, switches and automatic generation were inspected in the Technical application code at repository revision cf5241ad6, which the application owner has confirmed is the code running in the Technical application. Where this guidance and a manual differ, say which source says what.
Applies to: Vessel
Who can do it (role): None — no user action. Planned (scheduled) work orders are created by the ship system itself.
Prerequisites (switches, settings, job/component state):
- The instance must be a ship instance; the scan does not run on the office (shore) instance.
- The job must be active and attached to a component, with a frequency and a next due date (the job schedule shown in Components → Part C).
- The job must not already have an active work order — one active work order per job at a time.
Steps:
1. Nothing to do. The ship system runs the job-due scan once every 24 hours by default (interval configurable through JOB_DUE_SCAN_INTERVAL_MS) and creates a work order for every job that is due.
2. To see the result, open the 'Work Orders' sub-sub-module and use the status tabs: Scheduled (jobs planned with a future due date), Due (due within the current period), Overdue (past due date, not completed).
3. The work-order number is prefixed with the vessel code: <VESSELCODE>-<JOBCODE>-<COMPONENTCODE>-<YEAR>-<NNN>.
Exceptions / edge cases:
- If a job already has an active work order, the scan does not create another one for it.
- A work order can be generated ahead of the scan for one job with 'Generate WO' on the Components page — see office-generate-wo-per-job.md.
- On the office instance no scan runs. Office-side generation is done by a user in one of two ways: 'Generate Now' for a whole vessel (Sail Admin AND the vessel switch) — see office-generate-now.md — or per-job 'Generate WO' from Components (the vessel switch) — see office-generate-wo-per-job.md.
Where the manual differs:
- The June manuals do not describe automatic generation at all. They describe the Scheduled / Due / Overdue tabs and the job frequency / next due date but never say that the system creates the work orders. Recorded in CONFLICTS.md.
Related procedures: office-generate-now.md · office-generate-wo-per-job.md · unplanned-wo.md

---

[5] (Technical - PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — Introduction (p.28))
* Work Orders (WO) are a structured instruction used onboard to plan, execute, and record maintenance activities for equipment and systems. It serves as the primary control mechanism to ensure that all maintenance tasks are carried out systematically, safely, and in compliance with company and regulatory requirements.

FULL ANSWER:
From the office, the supported ways are:

1. **Generate Now for a vessel:** Only a **Sail Admin** can do this. The selected vessel’s **office work-order generation** switch must be ON. If it is OFF, the server refuses the request with: **“Office work-order generation is not enabled for this vessel.”** Other office roles are refused with a role message. The switch is enabled on **Lead Time & Grace Period Settings** and is OFF by default.

2. **Per-job “Generate WO”:** No special role is required beyond normal sign-in and vessel access, but the vessel’s office-generation switch must be ON. If it is OFF, this office path cannot be used.

3. **Unplanned work order:** No special role or office-generation switch is required; normal sign-in and vessel access still apply.

The June PMS manuals do not describe the Sail Admin restriction or the switch behavior. Those details come from the draft, code-derived guidance; the recent operational notes also confirm the Sail Admin and per-vessel-switch rules.

**Source:**  
- Generate Now: *Technical — Recent Updates & Changed Behaviours (Operational) Notes*, §1.1.14.6; draft code-derived guidance, *Office “Generate Now” — generate a vessel’s due work orders from the office*.  
- Per-job “Generate WO”: draft code-derived guidance, *How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order*.  
- Unplanned work order: draft code-derived guidance, *How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order*.

[5] (Technical - PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — Introduction (p.28))
* Work Orders (WO) are a structured instruction used onboard to plan, execute, and record maintenance activities for equipment and systems. It serves as the primary control mechanism to ensure that all maintenance tasks are carried out systematically, safely, and in compliance with company and regulatory requirements.

FULL ANSWER:
The excerpts support three office ways to generate work orders:

1. **Generate Now for a vessel**
   1. Only a **Sail Admin** can use this office function.
   2. Select one vessel on the **Work Orders** screen and click **Generate Now**.
   3. The vessel’s **office work-order generation** switch must be **ON**. It is OFF by default and is enabled on **Lead Time & Grace Period Settings**.
   4. If the switch is OFF, the server refuses the request with: **“Office work-order generation is not enabled for this vessel.”**
   5. The generation applies only to that vessel and creates due jobs that have no active work order.

2. **Generate WO for one job**
   1. Open **Components**, select the vessel in the office, open the component, go to **Part C**, and click **Generate WO**.
   2. Choose **Planning**, **Breakdown**, or **Other**.
   3. There is **no role check**, but normal sign-in and vessel access apply.
   4. In the office, the vessel’s generation switch must be **ON**. The job must be active and must not already have an active work order.
   5. If the switch is OFF, this office generation path is not permitted; the excerpts do not specify its exact refusal message.

3. **Create an unplanned work order**
   1. Open **Work Orders**, select the vessel in the office, and click **+ Unplanned W.O**.
   2. Complete **Part A** and save it.
   3. Complete **Part B** and submit the work order.
   4. There is **no role check** and **no office generation switch is required**; normal sign-in and vessel access apply.

The office switch does **not** control the ship’s automatic daily generation. On a ship instance, the system creates planned work orders automatically, without user action. The cited office-role and switch rules come from draft, code-derived guidance; the June published manual does not specify them.

**Source:**
- “How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order,” draft code-derived guidance, sections “Who can do it,” “Prerequisites,” and “Steps.”
- “Office ‘Generate Now’ — generate a vessel’s due work orders from the office,” draft code-derived guidance, sections “Who can do it,” “Prerequisites,” and “Steps.”
- “Planned work orders — generated automatically by the ship’s daily job-due scan,” draft code-derived guidance, sections “Prerequisites” and “Exceptions / edge cases.”
- “PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026,” Introduction, p. 28.

[1] (Technical - Recent Updates & Changed Behaviours (Operational) Notes — 1.1.14.6 Office Generation of Work Orders Is a Per-Vessel Switch)
* Only a Sail Admin can generate work orders directly from the office, and only for a vessel whose 'office work-order generation' switch is enabled (off by default). The switch is on the 'Lead Time & Grace Period Settings' screen.

* With the switch off, the Sail Admin is told that office work-order generation is not enabled for the vessel and where to enable it. Other office roles are refused with a role message.

* Ships always generate their own work orders on their daily scan, regardless of this switch.

* The office 'Generate Now' button (Work Orders screen, with one vessel selected) runs an immediate, vessel-scoped generation instead of waiting for the daily sweep, subject to the same switch and role rule.

---

[2] (Technical - KB pilot: Office 'Generate Now' — generate a vessel's due work orders from the office.md — Office 'Generate Now' — generate a vessel's due work orders from the office)
Provenance note: draft code-derived guidance, not a published manual. 3 statement(s) come from the June PMS manuals; 10 statement(s) about roles, switches and automatic generation were inspected in the Technical application code at repository revision cf5241ad6, which the application owner has confirmed is the code running in the Technical application. Where this guidance and a manual differ, say which source says what.
Applies to: Office
Who can do it (role): Sail Admin only. Any other office role is refused by the server with "Only a Sail Admin may generate work orders directly from the office."
Prerequisites (switches, settings, job/component state):
- The selected vessel's 'office work-order generation' switch must be ON. It is OFF by default for every vessel; a Sail Admin enables it per vessel on the 'Lead Time & Grace Period Settings' screen. With the switch off the server refuses with "Office work-order generation is not enabled for this vessel."
- One specific vessel must be selected on the Work Orders screen — the button is disabled for 'All' or 'My' vessels.
- Only jobs that are due and have no active work order are generated — the same rules as the ship's scan.
Steps:
1. Open the 'Work Orders' sub-sub-module and select the vessel from the 'Vessel' dropdown.
2. Click 'Generate Now' in the toolbar of the Work Orders screen (next to 'Planner' and 'Export').
3. The server runs the generation sweep for that vessel only and returns; the new work orders appear under the Scheduled / Due / Overdue tabs.
Exceptions / edge cases:
- The button is shown to Sail Admin, Client Admin and Head of Dept on the office instance, but the server accepts Sail Admin only; the other two roles see a button whose every click is refused. Recorded in CONFLICTS.md (existing standalone note).
- On a ship instance this gate always allows generation (the ship's own scan is the normal path).
- 'Generate Now' does NOT apply to the per-job 'Generate WO' button on the Components page, which has its own, different condition — see office-generate-wo-per-job.md.
Where the manual differs:
- The June manuals do not mention 'Generate Now', the Sail Admin restriction or the per-vessel switch. Recorded in CONFLICTS.md.
Related procedures: planned-wo-ship-daily-scan.md · office-generate-wo-per-job.md · unplanned-wo.md

---

[3] (Technical - KB pilot: How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order.md — How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order)
Provenance note: draft code-derived guidance, not a published manual. 3 statement(s) come from the June PMS manuals; 7 statement(s) about roles, switches and automatic generation were inspected in the Technical application code at repository revision cf5241ad6, which the application owner has confirmed is the code running in the Technical application. Where this guidance and a manual differ, say which source says what.
Applies to: Both
Who can do it (role): Depends on the way — (1) planned: nobody, the ship system creates them; office 'Generate Now': Sail Admin only; (2) per-job 'Generate WO': no role check; (3) unplanned: no role check. Normal sign-in and vessel access apply to all.
Prerequisites (switches, settings, job/component state): Office 'Generate Now' and office per-job 'Generate WO' both need the vessel's 'office work-order generation' switch ON (off by default, set by a Sail Admin on 'Lead Time & Grace Period Settings'). Unplanned needs no switch. A job with an active work order cannot get another one.
Steps:
1. Planned (scheduled) work orders — generated by the system from each job's frequency and next due date. ON THE SHIP: automatic, once a day, no user action. IN THE OFFICE: nothing runs automatically; a Sail Admin can click 'Generate Now' on the Work Orders screen for one vessel, only if that vessel's switch is on. Details: planned-wo-ship-daily-scan.md and office-generate-now.md.
2. One job on demand — Components → (Office: select the vessel) → open the component → Part C → 'Generate WO' → choose the reason (Planning / Breakdown / Other). No role check on this path (sign-in and vessel access still apply); in the office only if the vessel switch is on; the job must be active and must not already have an active work order. Details: office-generate-wo-per-job.md.
3. Unplanned work — Work Orders → (Office: select the vessel) → '+ Unplanned W.O' → Part A, Save → Part B, Submit Work Order. No role check on this path, no switch. Details: unplanned-wo.md.
Exceptions / edge cases: The Sail Admin requirement belongs to 'Generate Now' only; it does not apply to per-job 'Generate WO' or to unplanned work orders.
Where the manual differs: The June manuals describe only ways 2 and 3 and state no office conditions; automatic ship generation and 'Generate Now' are absent from them. See CONFLICTS.md.
Related procedures: planned-wo-ship-daily-scan.md · office-generate-now.md · office-generate-wo-per-job.md · unplanned-wo.md

---

[4] (Technical - KB pilot: Planned work orders — generated automatically by the ship's daily job-due scan.md — Planned work orders — generated automatically by the ship's daily job-due scan)
Provenance note: draft code-derived guidance, not a published manual. 6 statement(s) come from the June PMS manuals; 7 statement(s) about roles, switches and automatic generation were inspected in the Technical application code at repository revision cf5241ad6, which the application owner has confirmed is the code running in the Technical application. Where this guidance and a manual differ, say which source says what.
Applies to: Vessel
Who can do it (role): None — no user action. Planned (scheduled) work orders are created by the ship system itself.
Prerequisites (switches, settings, job/component state):
- The instance must be a ship instance; the scan does not run on the office (shore) instance.
- The job must be active and attached to a component, with a frequency and a next due date (the job schedule shown in Components → Part C).
- The job must not already have an active work order — one active work order per job at a time.
Steps:
1. Nothing to do. The ship system runs the job-due scan once every 24 hours by default (interval configurable through JOB_DUE_SCAN_INTERVAL_MS) and creates a work order for every job that is due.
2. To see the result, open the 'Work Orders' sub-sub-module and use the status tabs: Scheduled (jobs planned with a future due date), Due (due within the current period), Overdue (past due date, not completed).
3. The work-order number is prefixed with the vessel code: <VESSELCODE>-<JOBCODE>-<COMPONENTCODE>-<YEAR>-<NNN>.
Exceptions / edge cases:
- If a job already has an active work order, the scan does not create another one for it.
- A work order can be generated ahead of the scan for one job with 'Generate WO' on the Components page — see office-generate-wo-per-job.md.
- On the office instance no scan runs. Office-side generation is done by a user in one of two ways: 'Generate Now' for a whole vessel (Sail Admin AND the vessel switch) — see office-generate-now.md — or per-job 'Generate WO' from Components (the vessel switch) — see office-generate-wo-per-job.md.
Where the manual differs:
- The June manuals do not describe automatic generation at all. They describe the Scheduled / Due / Overdue tabs and the job frequency / next due date but never say that the system creates the work orders. Recorded in CONFLICTS.md.
Related procedures: office-generate-now.md · office-generate-wo-per-job.md · unplanned-wo.md

---

[5] (Technical - PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — Introduction (p.28))
* Work Orders (WO) are a structured instruction used onboard to plan, execute, and record maintenance activities for equipment and systems. It serves as the primary control mechanism to ensure that all maintenance tasks are carried out systematically, safely, and in compliance with company and regulatory requirements.

FULL ANSWER:
The excerpts support two office ways to generate work orders:

1. **Generate Now for a vessel**
   1. Only a **Sail Admin** may generate work orders directly from the office.
   2. Select **one vessel** on the Work Orders screen. The vessel’s **office work-order generation** switch must be **ON**; it is OFF by default and is enabled on **Lead Time & Grace Period Settings**.
   3. Click **Generate Now**. Generation runs immediately for that vessel, subject to the normal due-job and active-work-order rules.
   4. If the switch is OFF, generation is refused with a message that office work-order generation is not enabled for that vessel and indicating where to enable it. Other office roles are refused with a role message.

2. **Per-job “Generate WO” from Components**
   1. No specific role check applies, although normal sign-in and vessel access are required.
   2. In the office, the vessel’s **office work-order generation** switch must be **ON**. The job must be active and must not already have an active work order.
   3. Open **Components → component → Part C → Generate WO**, then choose **Planning**, **Breakdown**, or **Other**.
   4. The excerpts do not state the exact message or UI response when this switch is OFF; they state only that the switch is required.

Ships generate their own planned work orders through the daily scan; the office switch does not control that ship-side process.

**Source:**  
- Generate Now: *Technical - Recent Updates & Changed Behaviours (Operational) Notes*, §1.1.14.6; draft code-derived guidance, *Office “Generate Now” — generate a vessel’s due work orders from the office*.  
- Per-job “Generate WO”: draft code-derived guidance, *How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order*.

[4] (Technical - Recent Updates & Changed Behaviours (Operational) Notes — 1.1.14.6 Office Generation of Work Orders — Conditions by Action)
* Office 'Generate Now' (Work Orders screen, one vessel selected) — runs an immediate, vessel-scoped generation instead of waiting for the daily sweep. Two conditions apply, both on the server: the caller's role must be Sail Admin, and that vessel's 'office work-order generation' switch must be enabled. The switch is off by default and is set on the 'Lead Time & Grace Period Settings' screen. [code: workOrderController.generateNow → workOrderGenerationGate.evaluateDirectGeneration; PROVISIONING_ROLE = 'Sail Admin'; isOfficeWoGenerationEnabled]

* Refusals for 'Generate Now' are distinct. A caller who is not a Sail Admin is refused with: "Only a Sail Admin may generate work orders directly from the office." A Sail Admin acting on a vessel whose switch is off is refused with: "Office work-order generation is not enabled for this vessel. A Sail Admin can enable it per vessel on the Lead Time & Grace Period Settings screen." A vessel whose provisioning state cannot be read is refused as well — the check fails closed. FULL ANSWER:
From the office, work orders can be created in three ways:

1. **Whole-vessel “Generate Now”**
   1. Only a **Sail Admin** can use it; other roles are refused by the server.
   2. Select one vessel on **Work Orders** and click **Generate Now**.
   3. The vessel’s **office work-order generation** switch must be ON. If it is OFF, the request is refused with: **“Office work-order generation is not enabled for this vessel.”**
   4. The switch is enabled per vessel by a Sail Admin on **Lead Time & Grace Period Settings**. The button may be visible to Client Admin and Head of Dept, but their clicks are refused.

2. **Per-job “Generate WO”**
   1. Any signed-in user with access to the vessel may use it; there is no Sail Admin-only check.
   2. The vessel’s office-generation switch must be ON.
   3. The job must be active and must not already have an active work order. The reason must be **Planning**, **Breakdown**, or **Other**.
   4. If the switch is OFF, this action is refused.

3. **“+ Unplanned W.O”**
   1. A normally signed-in user with vessel access may create it; no Sail Admin rule applies.
   2. The office-generation switch is not required, so this path remains available when the switch is OFF.

These role and switch rules come from draft, code-derived guidance at revision **cf5241ad6**; the June manuals do not describe these office conditions.

**Source:**  
- Whole-vessel “Generate Now”: *KB pilot: Office “Generate Now” — generate a vessel’s due work orders from the office*; *Recent Updates & Changed Behaviours, 1.1.14.6 — Conditions by Action*.  
- Per-job “Generate WO”: *KB pilot: How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order*; *Recent Updates & Changed Behaviours, 1.1.14.6 — Conditions by Action*.  
- “+ Unplanned W.O”: *KB pilot: How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order*; *Recent Updates & Changed Behaviours, 1.1.14.6 — Conditions by Action*.

3. Unplanned work — Work Orders → (Office: select the vessel) → '+ Unplanned W.O' → Part A, Save → Part B, Submit Work Order. No role check on this path, no switch. Details: unplanned-wo.md.
Exceptions / edge cases: The Sail Admin requirement belongs to 'Generate Now' only; it does not apply to per-job 'Generate WO' or to unplanned work orders.
Where the manual differs: The June manuals describe only ways 2 and 3 and state no office conditions; automatic ship generation and 'Generate Now' are absent from them. See CONFLICTS.md.
Related procedures: planned-wo-ship-daily-scan.md · office-generate-now.md · office-generate-wo-per-job.md · unplanned-wo.md

* Office 'Generate Now' (Work Orders screen, one vessel selected) — runs an immediate, vessel-scoped generation instead of waiting for the daily sweep. Two conditions apply, both on the server: the caller's role must be Sail Admin, and that vessel's 'office work-order generation' switch must be enabled. The switch is off by default and is set on the 'Lead Time & Grace Period Settings' screen. [code: workOrderController.generateNow → workOrderGenerationGate.evaluateDirectGeneration; PROVISIONING_ROLE = 'Sail Admin'; isOfficeWoGenerationEnabled]

* Refusals for 'Generate Now' are distinct. A caller who is not a Sail Admin is refused with: "Only a Sail Admin may generate work orders directly from the office." A Sail Admin acting on a vessel whose switch is off is refused with: "Office work-order generation is not enabled for this vessel. A Sail Admin can enable it per vessel on the Lead Time & Grace Period Settings screen." A vessel whose provisioning state cannot be read is refused as well — the check fails closed. ---

[5] (Technical - KB pilot: Planned work orders — generated automatically by the ship's daily job-due scan.md — Planned work orders — generated automatically by the ship's daily job-due scan)
Provenance note: draft code-derived guidance, not a published manual. 6 statement(s) come from the June PMS manuals; 7 statement(s) about roles, switches and automatic generation were inspected in the Technical application code at repository revision cf5241ad6, which the application owner has confirmed is the code running in the Technical application. Where this guidance and a manual differ, say which source says what.
Applies to: Vessel
Who can do it (role): None — no user action. Planned (scheduled) work orders are created by the ship system itself.
Prerequisites (switches, settings, job/component state):
- The instance must be a ship instance; the scan does not run on the office (shore) instance.
- The job must be active and attached to a component, with a frequency and a next due date (the job schedule shown in Components → Part C).
- The job must not already have an active work order — one active work order per job at a time.
Steps:
1. Nothing to do. The ship system runs the job-due scan once every 24 hours by default (interval configurable through JOB_DUE_SCAN_INTERVAL_MS) and creates a work order for every job that is due.
2. To see the result, open the 'Work Orders' sub-sub-module and use the status tabs: Scheduled (jobs planned with a future due date), Due (due within the current period), Overdue (past due date, not completed).
3. The work-order number is prefixed with the vessel code: <VESSELCODE>-<JOBCODE>-<COMPONENTCODE>-<YEAR>-<NNN>.
Exceptions / edge cases:
- If a job already has an active work order, the scan does not create another one for it.
- A work order can be generated ahead of the scan for one job with 'Generate WO' on the Components page — see office-generate-wo-per-job.md.
- On the office instance no scan runs. Office-side generation is done by a user in one of two ways: 'Generate Now' for a whole vessel (Sail Admin AND the vessel switch) — see office-generate-now.md — or per-job 'Generate WO' from Components (the vessel switch) — see office-generate-wo-per-job.md.
Where the manual differs:
- The June manuals do not describe automatic generation at all. They describe the Scheduled / Due / Overdue tabs and the job frequency / next due date but never say that the system creates the work orders. Recorded in CONFLICTS.md.
Related procedures: office-generate-now.md · office-generate-wo-per-job.md · unplanned-wo.md

FULL ANSWER:
From the office, work orders can be generated in three ways:

1. **Generate Now for a whole vessel:** Only a **Sail Admin** can do this. The selected vessel’s **office work-order generation** switch must be ON. If it is OFF, the server refuses the request with: **“Office work-order generation is not enabled for this vessel.”**

2. **Per-job “Generate WO” from Components:** **Any signed-in user with access to the vessel** may use this; there is no role-specific check. However, the vessel’s switch must be ON, and the job must be active and have no active work order. If the switch is OFF, this office generation path cannot be used.

3. **“+ Unplanned W.O” from Work Orders:** Normal sign-in and vessel access are required, but **the office generation switch and Sail Admin restriction do not apply**. The switch being OFF does not prevent unplanned work orders.

These role and switch conditions come from draft, code-derived guidance based on application revision `cf5241ad6`; the June manuals do not state these office conditions.

**Source:**  
- Whole-vessel **Generate Now** — *KB pilot: Office “Generate Now” — generate a vessel’s due work orders from the office*, Office “Generate Now” section; *Recent Updates & Changed Behaviours*, §1.1.14.6.  
- Per-job **Generate WO** — *How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order*, “One job on demand”; *Recent Updates & Changed Behaviours*, §1.1.14.6.  
- **Unplanned W.O** — *How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order*, “Unplanned work”.

FULL ANSWER:
From the office, work orders can be created in three ways:

1. **Generate Now for a whole vessel**
   - Only a **Sail Admin** can use it.
   - A specific vessel must be selected; **All** and **My vessels** are not allowed.
   - The vessel’s **office work-order generation** switch must be ON. If it is OFF, the server refuses the request with: **“Office work-order generation is not enabled for this vessel.”**
   - The switch is enabled per vessel on **Lead Time & Grace Period Settings**.

2. **Per-job “Generate WO” from Components**
   - **Any signed-in user with access to the vessel** may use it; there is no action-specific role check.
   - The vessel’s switch must be ON. If it is OFF, this generation path cannot be used.
   - The job must be active and must not already have an active work order. The reason must be **Planning, Breakdown, or Other**.

3. **“+ Unplanned W.O” from Work Orders**
   - Normal sign-in and vessel access apply; there is no Sail Admin requirement.
   - The office generation switch does **not** apply, so an OFF switch does not block this path.

The office does not generate planned work orders automatically; automatic daily generation is a ship-instance process.

[5] (Technical - PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific_R3_08.07.2026 — Introduction (p.23))
* Work Orders (WO) is a structured instruction used onboard to plan, execute, and record maintenance activities for equipment and systems. It serves as the primary control mechanism to ensure that all maintenance tasks are carried out systematically, safely, and in compliance with company and regulatory requirements.

FULL ANSWER:
The excerpts support three ways to create a work order:

1. **Planned, scheduled work orders**
   - **On the ship:** the system creates them automatically once a day from each job’s frequency and next due date. No user action or role is required.
   - **In the office:** nothing runs automatically. A **Sail Admin** can click **Generate Now** on the Work Orders screen for one vessel, but the vessel’s **office work-order generation** switch must be ON. The switch is set in **Lead Time & Grace Period Settings** and is off by default.
   - A job with an active work order cannot receive another active work order.

2. **One job on demand**
   1. Go to **Components**.
   2. In the office, select the vessel.
   3. Open the component, go to **Part C**, and click **Generate WO**.
   4. Select the reason: **Planning**, **Breakdown**, or **Other**.
   - No special role check applies, but normal sign-in and vessel access are required.
   - In the office, the vessel’s **office work-order generation** switch must be ON.
   - The job must be active and must not already have an active work order.

3. **Unplanned work**
   1. Go to **Work Orders**.
   2. In the office, select the vessel.
   3. Click **+ Unplanned W.O**.
   4. Complete the required details in **Part A** and click **Save** to store the form as a draft.
   5. Complete **Part B** and click **Submit Work Order**.
   - No special role check or generation switch is required; normal sign-in and vessel access still apply.

The planned-generation and office **Generate Now** details come from draft, code-derived guidance. The published June manuals describe the per-job and unplanned procedures but do not include the automatic ship generation or office conditions.

**Source:**  
- Planned scheduled generation — draft code-derived guidance, *How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order*, planned work orders.  
- One job on demand — draft code-derived guidance, *How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order*, per-job **Generate WO**.  
- Unplanned work — *PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026*, §1.1.5.2, p.29; *PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific_R3_08.07.2026*, §1.1.5.2, p.24.

Applies to: Both
Who can do it (role): Depends on the way — (1) planned: nobody, the ship system creates them; office 'Generate Now': Sail Admin only; (2) per-job 'Generate WO': no role check; (3) unplanned: no role check. Normal sign-in and vessel access apply to all.
Prerequisites (switches, settings, job/component state): Office 'Generate Now' and office per-job 'Generate WO' both need the vessel's 'office work-order generation' switch ON (off by default, set by a Sail Admin on 'Lead Time & Grace Period Settings'). Unplanned needs no switch. A job with an active work order cannot get another one.
Steps:
1. Planned (scheduled) work orders — generated by the system from each job's frequency and next due date. ON THE SHIP: automatic, once a day, no user action. IN THE OFFICE: nothing runs automatically; a Sail Admin can click 'Generate Now' on the Work Orders screen for one vessel, only if that vessel's switch is on. Details: planned-wo-ship-daily-scan.md and office-generate-now.md.
2. One job on demand — Components → (Office: select the vessel) → open the component → Part C → 'Generate WO' → choose the reason (Planning / Breakdown / Other). No role check on this path (sign-in and vessel access still apply); in the office only if the vessel switch is on; the job must be active and must not already have an active work order. Details: office-generate-wo-per-job.md.
3. Unplanned work — Work Orders → (Office: select the vessel) → '+ Unplanned W.O' → Part A, Save → Part B, Submit Work Order. No role check on this path, no switch. Details: unplanned-wo.md.
Exceptions / edge cases: The Sail Admin requirement belongs to 'Generate Now' only; it does not apply to per-job 'Generate WO' or to unplanned work orders.
Where the manual differs: The June manuals describe only ways 2 and 3 and state no office conditions; automatic ship generation and 'Generate Now' are absent from them. See CONFLICTS.md.
Related procedures: planned-wo-ship-daily-scan.md · office-generate-now.md · office-generate-wo-per-job.md · unplanned-wo.md

It serves as the primary control mechanism to ensure that all maintenance tasks are carried out systematically, safely, and in compliance with company and regulatory requirements.

[5] (Technical - PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific_R3_08.07.2026 — Introduction (p.23))
* Work Orders (WO) is a structured instruction used onboard to plan, execute, and record maintenance activities for equipment and systems. It serves as the primary control mechanism to ensure that all maintenance tasks are carried out systematically, safely, and in compliance with company and regulatory requirements.

FULL ANSWER:
PMS supports these ways to create a work order:

1. **Planned, scheduled work orders**
   - The system generates them from each job’s frequency and next due date.
   - **Ship:** generated automatically once a day; no user action is required.
   - **Office:** nothing runs automatically. A **Sail Admin** can click **Generate Now** on the Work Orders screen for one vessel, but the vessel’s **office work-order generation** switch must be ON. The switch is set in **Lead Time & Grace Period Settings** and is OFF by default.
   - A job with an active work order cannot receive another one.

2. **One job on demand**
   - Go to **Components**. In the Office, first select the vessel.
   - Open the component, go to **Part C**, click **Generate WO**, and choose **Planning**, **Breakdown**, or **Other**.
   - The job must be active and must not already have an active work order.
   - There is no role check; normal sign-in and vessel access still apply.
   - In the Office, the vessel’s **office work-order generation** switch must be ON. This switch condition does not apply on the Ship.

3. **Unplanned work**
   - Go to **Work Orders**. In the Office, select the vessel.
   - Click **+ Unplanned W.O**.
   - Complete the required details in **Part A** and click **Save** to store the form as a draft.
   - Complete **Part B** and click **Submit Work Order**.
   - There is no role check and no generation switch is required; normal sign-in and vessel access still apply.

The planned-generation and office **Generate Now** details come from draft code-derived guidance, marked as not a published manual. The June published manuals describe only the on-demand and unplanned procedures and do not state the office conditions above.

**Source:**  
- Planned scheduled generation — *Technical - KB pilot: How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order*, “How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order” (draft code-derived guidance, revision cf5241ad).  
- One job on demand — same draft guidance, same section; related procedure `office-generate-wo-per-job.md`.  
- Unplanned work — *PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026*, §1.1.5.2, p.29; *PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific_R3_08.07.2026*, §1.1.5.2, p.24.

[5] (Technical - PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific_R3_08.07.2026 — Introduction (p.23))
* Work Orders (WO) is a structured instruction used onboard to plan, execute, and record maintenance activities for equipment and systems. It serves as the primary control mechanism to ensure that all maintenance tasks are carried out systematically, safely, and in compliance with company and regulatory requirements.

FULL ANSWER:
PMS supports three ways to create a work order:

1. **Planned/scheduled work order**
   - The ship system creates it automatically once daily from the job’s frequency and next due date; no user action is required.
   - In the office, nothing runs automatically. A **Sail Admin** can click **Generate Now** for one vessel, but only when that vessel’s **office work-order generation** switch is ON. The switch is set in **Lead Time & Grace Period Settings** and is off by default.
   - A job with an active work order cannot receive another one.

2. **One job on demand**
   - Go to **Components**; in the office, select the vessel.
   - Open the component, go to **Part C**, click **Generate WO**, and select **Planning**, **Breakdown**, or **Other**.
   - There is no role check, but normal sign-in and vessel access apply. The job must be active and have no active work order. In the office, the vessel’s **office work-order generation** switch must be ON.

3. **Unplanned work order**
   - Go to **Work Orders**; in the office, select the vessel.
   - Click **+ Unplanned W.O**.
   - Complete the required details in **Part A** and click **Save** to store the draft.
   - Complete **Part B** and click **Submit Work Order**.
   - No special switch or role check is required; normal sign-in and vessel access apply.

The first set of details comes from draft, code-derived guidance. The June published manuals describe the **one-job** and **unplanned** procedures, but do not describe automatic ship generation or the office conditions; they also do not state the office switch and role conditions.

**Source:**  
- Planned/scheduled: *How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order*, “Planned (scheduled) work orders” (draft code-derived guidance, revision cf5241ad6).  
- One job on demand: same guidance, “One job on demand”; *PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026*, §1.1.5.1 if applicable.  
- Unplanned: same guidance, “Unplanned work”; *PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026*, §1.1.5.2, p.29; *PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific_R3_08.07.2026*, §1.1.5.2, p.24.

Applies to: Both
Who can do it (role): Depends on the way — (1) planned: nobody, the ship system creates them; office 'Generate Now': Sail Admin only; (2) per-job 'Generate WO': no role check; (3) unplanned: no role check. Normal sign-in and vessel access apply to all.
