No role check on this path (sign-in and vessel access still apply); in the office only if the vessel switch is on; the job must be active and must not already have an active work order. Details: office-generate-wo-per-job.md.
3. Unplanned work — Work Orders → (Office: select the vessel) → '+ Unplanned W.O' → Part A, Save → Part B, Submit Work Order. No role check on this path, no switch. Details: unplanned-wo.md.
Exceptions / edge cases: The Sail Admin requirement belongs to 'Generate Now' only; it does not apply to per-job 'Generate WO' or to unplanned work orders.
Where the manual differs: The June manuals describe only ways 2 and 3 and state no office conditions; automatic ship generation and 'Generate Now' are absent from them. See CONFLICTS.md.

[5] (Technical - PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific_R3_08.07.2026 — Introduction (p.23))
* Work Orders (WO) is a structured instruction used onboard to plan, execute, and record maintenance activities for equipment and systems. It serves as the primary control mechanism to ensure that all maintenance tasks are carried out systematically, safely, and in compliance with company and regulatory requirements.

FULL ANSWER:
PMS supports three ways to create a work order:

1. **Planned, scheduled work orders**
   - The ship system creates them automatically once per day, based on each job’s frequency and next due date; no user action is required.
   - In the Office, nothing runs automatically. A **Sail Admin** can click **Generate Now** for one vessel, but only when that vessel’s **office work-order generation** switch is ON. The switch is set in **Lead Time & Grace Period Settings** and is OFF by default.
   - A job must not already have an active work order. Normal sign-in and vessel access apply.

2. **One job on demand**
   - Go to **Components**. In the Office, select the vessel, open the component, go to **Part C**, click **Generate WO**, and choose **Planning**, **Breakdown**, or **Other**.
   - There is no role check, but normal sign-in and vessel access apply. In the Office, the vessel’s **office work-order generation** switch must be ON. The job must be active and must not already have an active work order.

3. **Unplanned work**
   - Go to **Work Orders**. In the Office, select the vessel, then click **+ Unplanned W.O**.
   - Complete the required details in **Part A** and click **Save** to store the form as a draft. Complete **Part B** and click **Submit Work Order**.
   - There is no role check and no generation switch is required. Normal sign-in and vessel access apply. On the Ship, no vessel selection is required.

The planned-generation and Office conditions come from draft, code-derived guidance. The June published manuals describe the one-job and unplanned procedures, but do not describe automatic ship generation or the Office **Generate Now** function.

**Source:** Planned scheduled work orders — *Technical - KB pilot: How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order*, “Planned (scheduled) work orders” (draft code-derived guidance, repository revision cf5241ad6). One job on demand — same guidance, “One job on demand.” Unplanned work — *PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026*, §1.1.5.2, p.29; *PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific_R3_08.07.2026*, §1.1.5.2, p.24.

[4] (Technical - Cert. & Surveys User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — 1.2.1 SHIP’S CERTIFICATES (p.13))
**Introduction**

* The ‘**Ship’s Certificates**’ sub-submodule is used to manage the master list of certificate types maintained within the system. It enables authorized users to create, configure, and maintain certificate definitions, including certificate names, categories, groups, regulatory references, and applicability settings. The module also allows administrators to control the assignment of certificates at the company and vessel levels, ensuring that certificate requirements are standardized and consistently applied across the fleet.

---

[5] (Technical - Cert. & Surveys User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — 1.2.1.1 HOW TO ACCESS THE SHIP’S CERTIFICATES (p.13))
* Click on the ‘Admin’ sub-module. (Ref Figure 12)

* Click the ‘Ship’s Certificates’ sub-submodule to view certificate details; the ‘Master’ tab is displayed by default. (Ref Figure 12)

* Upon entering the ‘Ship’s Certificates’ sub-submodule, the system automatically opens the ‘Master’ tab by default, allowing users to view all certificate details:

 * **Master Tab**: The Master tab serves as the central repository for all certificate master records available in the system. It allows administrators to define and maintain certificate types, categories, groups, regulatory references, and certificate labels. This view establishes the standard certificate structure that can be applied across companies and vessels. (Ref Figure 12)

 * **Company Tab**: The Company tab displays certificate records configured for individual companies. It allows administrators to review company-specific certificate requirements, certificate labels, regulatory references, and company group assignments. This view helps ensure that the appropriate certificate masters are assigned and maintained at the company level. (Ref Figure 12)

 * **Vessel Tab**: The Vessel tab displays certificate records applicable

FULL ANSWER:
To add a brand-new certificate type to the **Master** list:

1. In the **Office Sail Admin**, click the **Admin** sub-module, then **Ship’s Certificates**. The **Master** tab opens by default.
2. Click **Edit**.
3. Click **New**.
4. Enter the required details in the new editable row:
   - Certificate Name
   - Category
   - Group
   - Requirement/Reference
   - Applicable to Company
   - Certificate Label  
   Mandatory fields must be completed. The system generates the **Master ID** automatically.
5. Click the **Save** icon.
6. Choose **Yes, Reorder** to place the certificate under its selected Category and Group, or **No, Keep at End** to leave it at the end of the list.

This function is for authorized users. The Master list also contains system-defined certificates that users cannot delete.

**Source:** Technical - Cert. & Surveys User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026, sections 1.2.1.1 “How to Access the Ship’s Certificates” (p.13), 1.2.1.4 “How to Add a New Master Certificate” (p.17), and Technical User Manual (p.18).

FULL ANSWER:
To add a brand-new certificate type to the **Master** list:

1. As an authorized user in the Office Admin module, click **Admin**, then **Ship’s Certificates**. The **Master** tab opens by default.
2. Click **Edit**.
3. Click **New** to add a certificate record.
4. Enter the required details in the new editable row:
   - Certificate Name
   - Category
   - Group
   - Requirement/Reference
   - Applicable to Company
   - Certificate Label
5. Click the **Save** icon.
6. Choose **Yes, Reorder** to place the certificate in its selected Category and Group, or **No, Keep at End** to leave it at the end of the table.

The Master list contains 71 system-defined certificates, which users cannot delete. Fleet-wide certificate assignment is a separate procedure and does not create a new Master certificate type.

[4] (Technical - Cert. & Surveys User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — 1.2.1 SHIP’S CERTIFICATES (p.13))
**Introduction**

* The ‘**Ship’s Certificates**’ sub-submodule is used to manage the master list of certificate types maintained within the system. It enables authorized users to create, configure, and maintain certificate definitions, including certificate names, categories, groups, regulatory references, and applicability settings. The module also allows administrators to control the assignment of certificates at the company and vessel levels, ensuring that certificate requirements are standardized and consistently applied across the fleet.

---

[5] (Technical - Cert. & Surveys User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — 1.2.1.1 HOW TO ACCESS THE SHIP’S CERTIFICATES (p.13))
* Click on the ‘Admin’ sub-module. (Ref Figure 12)

* Click the ‘Ship’s Certificates’ sub-submodule to view certificate details; the ‘Master’ tab is displayed by default. (Ref Figure 12)

* Upon entering the ‘Ship’s Certificates’ sub-submodule, the system automatically opens the ‘Master’ tab by default, allowing users to view all certificate details:

 * **Master Tab**: The Master tab serves as the central repository for all certificate master records available in the system. It allows administrators to define and maintain certificate types, categories, groups, regulatory references, and certificate labels. This view establishes the standard certificate structure that can be applied across companies and vessels. (Ref Figure 12)

 * **Company Tab**: The Company tab displays certificate records configured for individual companies. It allows administrators to review company-specific certificate requirements, certificate labels, regulatory references, and company group assignments. This view helps ensure that the appropriate certificate masters are assigned and maintained at the company level. (Ref Figure 12)

 * **Vessel Tab**: The Vessel tab displays certificate records applicable

FULL ANSWER:
1. In the **Admin** sub-module, click **Ship’s Certificates**. The **Master** tab opens by default.
2. Click **Edit**.
3. Click **New** to add a certificate record.
4. Enter the required details in the new editable row: **Certificate Name, Category, Group, Requirement/Reference, Applicable to Company,** and **Certificate Label**. The system generates a **Master ID** automatically.
5. Click the **Save** icon.
6. Choose **Yes, Reorder** to place the certificate in its selected Category and Group section, or **No, Keep at End** to leave it at the end of the table.

This is for an authorized user managing the Master certificate list. The excerpts also describe applying a certificate type fleet-wide, but do not provide the complete steps for that separate action.

**Source:** Technical - Cert. & Surveys User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026, sections 1.2.1.1 “How to Access the Ship’s Certificates” (p.13), 1.2.1.4 “How to Add a New Master Certificate” (p.17), and Technical User Manual (p.18).

FULL ANSWER:
To add a brand-new certificate type to the **Master** list:

1. In the **Office Sail Admin** module, click **Admin**, then **Ship’s Certificates**. The **Master** tab opens by default.
2. Click **Edit**.
3. Click **New**. The system adds an editable row at the bottom with an auto-generated **Master ID**.
4. Enter the required details:
   - Certificate Name
   - Category
   - Group
   - Requirement/Reference
   - Applicable to Company
   - Certificate Label
5. Click the **Save** icon.
6. Choose:
   - **Yes, Reorder** to move the record into the selected Category and Group section, or
   - **No, Keep at End** to leave it at the end of the table.

This is for authorized users managing the Master certificate list. The excerpts do not describe another procedure for adding a brand-new Master certificate type.

[5] (Technical - Cert. & Surveys User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — 1.1.4.4 HOW TO EDIT A SURVEYS (p.10))
* Refer to the ‘Certificates’ sub-sub-module for the edit process and follow the same steps.

(Cross-reference resolved: the steps for Surveys › How To Edit A Surveys are the same as section 1.1.3.4 'How To Edit A Certificate' under Certificates, page 8. They are:)
* Go to the 'Certificates' sub-sub module. (<u>Ref Figure 8</u>)

* Select the required vessel from the 'Vessel' dropdown. (<u>Ref Figure 8</u>)

* Click the 'Issue Date' cell and select the required date from the date picker. (<u>Ref Figure 8</u>)

* Choose the new date and press 'Enter' or click outside the field. The system immediately updates the record and refreshes the corresponding Last Edit/Upload timestamp. (<u>Ref Figure 8</u>)

Screenshot of the Certificates module with numbered callouts explaining the editing process: 1. Click on the 'Certificates' tab. 2. Select the vessel. 3. Enter the date by clicking the Issue Date cell. 4. Upon entering the date, the Timestamp auto-refreshes. A red note box explains color indicators for certificate status.

Figure 8

[!NOTE]
Follow the same procedure to update the **Expiry Date**, **Last Annual**, **Last Intermediate**, and **Endorsement Date** fields, where applicable.

FULL ANSWER:
The manual does not explicitly confirm whether the survey process is identical to the certificate process. It only instructs you to refer to the **Certificates** sub-sub-module for survey attachments.

For certificates, the documented process is:

1. Select the vessel from the **Vessel** dropdown.
2. Click the **Attachment** icon for the certificate record.
3. Upload a **PDF, JPG, or PNG** file.
4. Use **View** to preview or **Delete** to remove an attachment.
5. Click **Done** to save.
6. Each attachment must be **5 MB or smaller**. Unsupported types or larger files are rejected.

For surveys, the excerpt also states that a survey must be marked **Applicable** for the vessel in the Admin sub-module to appear under **Surveys**. The survey attachment excerpt does not provide separate steps or state explicitly that the process is the same as certificates.

**Source:**  
- Certificate attachments: *Cert. & Surveys User Manual*, §1.1.3.5, p.9.

[5] (Technical - Cert. & Surveys User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — 1.1.4.4 HOW TO EDIT A SURVEYS (p.10))

* Go to the 'Certificates' sub-sub module. (<u>Ref Figure 8</u>)

* Select the required vessel from the 'Vessel' dropdown. (<u>Ref Figure 8</u>)

* Click the 'Issue Date' cell and select the required date from the date picker. (<u>Ref Figure 8</u>)

FULL ANSWER:
For **survey attachments**, the manual says to refer to the **Certificates** sub-sub-module and follow its instructions. The survey excerpt does not provide separate survey-specific screen steps, so it does not identify any difference from the certificate process.

The documented attachment actions are:

1. Select the vessel from the **Vessel** dropdown.
2. Click the **Attachment** icon for the relevant record.
3. In the attachment window, click the upload area and select a **PDF, JPG, or PNG** file.
4. Use **View** to preview an attachment or **Delete** to remove it.
5. Click **Done** to save.
6. Each file must be no larger than **5 MB**. Unsupported types or larger files are rejected with a validation message.
7. If the survey is not listed, its master entry must be marked **Applicable** for that vessel in the Admin sub-module.

**Source:**  
- Survey attachments: *Technical - Cert. & Surveys User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026*, §1.1.4.5, p.11.  
- Attachment actions and limits: same manual, §1.1.3.5, p.9.

FULL ANSWER:
For survey attachments, the manual instructs you to refer to the **Certificates** sub-sub-module and follow its instructions. However, the survey section does not provide a resolved, step-by-step procedure, so the documentation does not explicitly confirm whether the interface is different.

The documented attachment procedure for a certificate is:

1. Select the vessel from the **Vessel** dropdown.
2. Click the **Attachment** icon in the record’s **Actions** column.
3. In the attachment window, click the upload area and select a **PDF, JPG, or PNG** file.
4. Use **View** to preview an attachment or **Delete** to remove it.
5. Click **Done** to save the changes.
6. Each attachment must be **no larger than 5 MB**. Larger files or unsupported formats cannot be uploaded.
7. If the survey is not listed for the vessel, its corresponding master entry must be marked **Applicable** for that vessel in the **Admin** sub-module.

**Source:**  
- Survey attachments: *Cert. & Surveys User Manual*, §1.1.4.5, p.11.  
- Detailed attachment actions: *Cert. & Surveys User Manual*, §1.1.3.5, p.9.

FULL ANSWER:
The survey attachment process is not different according to the manual. The **Surveys** section directs you to the **Certificates** sub-sub-module and to follow its attachment steps:

1. Select the required vessel from the **Vessel** dropdown.
2. Click the **Attachment** icon in the survey record’s **Actions** column.

4. Use **View** to preview an attachment or **Delete** to remove it.
5. Click **Done** to save the changes.

Each attachment may be up to **5 MB**. Larger files or unsupported formats are rejected. If the survey is not listed for the vessel, its corresponding master entry is not marked **Applicable** in the Admin sub-module.

**Source:** Technical - Cert. & Surveys User Manual for Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026, §1.1.4.5 p.11 and §1.1.3.5 p.9.

<mark>☞ If a survey is not available under the **'Surveys'** section for a vessel, it indicates that the corresponding master entry is not marked as **'Applicable'** for that vessel in the Admin sub-module.</mark>

---

* Refer to the ‘Certificates’ sub-sub-module for the edit process and follow the same steps.

(Cross-reference resolved: the steps for Surveys › How To Edit A Surveys are the same as section 1.1.3.4 'How To Edit A Certificate' under Certificates, page 8. They are:)
* Go to the 'Certificates' sub-sub module. (<u>Ref Figure 8</u>)

* Select the required vessel from the 'Vessel' dropdown. (<u>Ref Figure 8</u>)

* Click the 'Issue Date' cell and select the required date from the date picker. (<u>Ref Figure 8</u>)

* Choose the new date and press 'Enter' or click outside the field. The system immediately updates the record and refreshes the corresponding Last Edit/Upload timestamp. (<u>Ref Figure 8</u>)

Screenshot of the Certificates module with numbered callouts explaining the editing process: 1. Click on the 'Certificates' tab. 2. Select the vessel. 3. Enter the date by clicking the Issue Date cell. 4. Upon entering the date, the Timestamp auto-refreshes. A red note box explains color indicators for certificate status.

Figure 8

[!NOTE]
Follow the same procedure to update the **Expiry Date**, **Last Annual**, **Last Intermediate**, and **Endorsement Date** fields, where applicable.

FULL ANSWER:
For **survey attachments**, the manual only says to refer to the **Certificates** sub-sub-module for adding or viewing attachments and follow the same steps. It does not provide a resolved, detailed survey procedure, so the Technical documentation does not establish whether the process differs from certificates.

The documented **certificate** attachment process is:

1. Select the vessel from the **Vessel** dropdown.
2. Click the **Attachment** icon for the certificate record.
3. Upload a **PDF, JPG, or PNG** file.
4. Use **View** to preview or **Delete** to remove an attachment.
5. Click **Done** to save. Each file must be no larger than **5 MB**.
6. If the survey or certificate is unavailable, its master entry must be marked **Applicable** for that vessel in the Admin sub-module.

**Source:**  
- Survey attachments: *Technical - Cert. & Surveys User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026*, §1.1.4.5, p.11.  
- Certificate attachments: same manual, §1.1.3.5, p.9.

---

[3] (Crewing - Crewing User Manual R2_10.06.2026 — Introduction (p.45))
* The Crew Promotion module provides a centralized view of crew members who are eligible or being considered for promotion. It enables users to monitor promotion readiness, review qualification requirements, track checklist progress, and manage the approval workflow based on defined criteria such as licenses, age, sea service, training, recommendations, CES scores, and other competency requirements.

---

[4] (Crewing - Crewing User Manual R2_10.06.2026 — Document (p.100))
* The screen below appears after clicking the 'Edit' icon. (Ref Figure 127)

* Enter and add all required information in 'Part A' and 'Part B' section before proceeding. (Ref Figure 127)

* Click the 'Save Form' button to save the Promotion Review Form configuration. (Ref Figure 127)

screenshot: Promotion Review Form - Form Builder interface showing configuration options for promotion criteria and checklist

Figure 127

<mark>icon: pointing hand After the form is saved, promotion form can be filled from the 'Promotion' sub-module.</mark>

---

[5] (Crewing - Crewing User Manual R2_10.06.2026 — 1.6.1.1 HOW TO ACCESS PROMOTIONS (p.45))
* Click on the ‘Promotions’ submodule, the system automatically navigates to the ‘All’ sub-submodule as the default view. <u>(Ref Figure 50)</u>

screenshot_from_computer: Crew Promotion dashboard showing the Promotions submodule selected. An annotation box points to the Promotions icon and states: 1. Click the 'Promotions' sub-module to open the 'All' sub-submodule by default module.

<a id="figure-50"></a>Figure 50

FULL ANSWER:
No. The promotion review form must first be created and released through the **Admin** sub-module.

1. In **Admin**, configure the form by entering the required information in **Part A** and **Part B**.
2. Click **Save Form**.
3. After the form is saved and released, it becomes available in the **Promotion** sub-module.
4. In **Promotion**, open **All**, select the crew member, and click **Edit** to review the promotion record.

**Source:**
- **Admin form configuration and availability:** *Crewing User Manual R2_10.06.2026*, **Document**, p.100; **HOW TO EXECUTE A PROMOTION**, p.48.
- **Opening the promotion review form:** *Crewing User Manual R2_10.06.2026*, **HOW TO ACCESS PROMOTIONS**, p.45; **HOW TO REVIEW A PROMOTION RECORD**, p.46.

MANUALS — CASE crewing-3
QUESTION: On the Crew Due/Overdue timeline, what does it mean when a crew member's bar shows as green versus yellow versus red?
JUDGING RULE: must ['active or ongoing onboard', 'approaching the relief due', 'overdue for relief']; must_not ['on leave', 'awaiting sign-on']; expected Crewing - Crewing User Manual R2_10.06.2026.pdf p[39]. Evidence: Note: Timeline color code represents the following meaning: * Green bar: Indicates the active or ongoing onboard service period. * Yellow bar: Indicates the crew member is approaching the relief due period. * Red bar: Indicates the crew member is overdue for relief.
PER-RUN (current judges): E0 [False, False, False]  E1 [False, True, False]

CAPTURED MODEL INPUT (the excerpts exactly as sent):
[1] (Crewing - Crewing User Manual R2_10.06.2026 — 1.5.1.1 HOW TO ACCESS CREW DUE / OVERDUE STATUS (p.39))
* Click on the ‘Rotation’ submodule, the system automatically navigates to the ‘Due’ sub-sub-module as the default view. <u>(Ref Figure 42)</u>

screenshot: Crew Due/ Overdue interface showing timeline view with color-coded bars

Note: Timeline color code represents the following meaning:

* Green bar: Indicates the active or ongoing onboard service period.

* Yellow bar: Indicates the crew member is approaching the relief due period.

* Red bar: Indicates the crew member is overdue for relief.

Figure 42

---

[2] (Crewing - Crewing User Manual R2_10.06.2026 — SAIL - CREWING MODULE USER MANUAL (p.41))
tus displays "Proposed" along with the vessel name.

**Purple (Deployed):** Indicates that the crew member has been deployed and is awaiting sign-on. On hover, the status displays "Deployed" along with the vessel name.

**Red (On Board):** Indicates that the crew member is either In Transit or has Signed On to the vessel.

---

[3] (Crewing - Crewing User Manual R2_10.06.2026 — Introduction (p.39))
* The Crew Due/Overdue section offers a timeline-based overview of crew relief schedules, enabling quick identification of upcoming and overdue cases for efficient crew planning.

---

[4] (Crewing - Crewing User Manual R2_10.06.2026 — SAIL - CREWING MODULE USER MANUAL (p.27))
* The screen below appears after clicking the 'Edit' icon to view a crew record. (Ref Figure 26)

* The Crew Profile Dashboard provides a comprehensive overview of the selected crew member, including status, experience, rank progression, certifications, and assignment timeline. (Ref Figure 26)

* Use the sidebar navigation to access the required section and update and save the records accordingly. (Ref Figure 26)

screenshot: Crew Profile Dashboard interface showing crew information sections like Status, Experience, Timeline, Rank, Appraisals, Ship Type, and Promotion, with callouts for overviewing crew information and moving between sections.

Figure 26

---

[5] (Crewing - Crewing User Manual R2_10.06.2026 — SAIL - CREWING MODULE USER MANUAL (p.72))
* The Vessel Status Overview section provides a summarized view of vessel operational and compliance status based on review, response, rest hours, and data completeness. (Ref Figure 86)

 * **O/Due Vessel Review:** Displays vessels with overdue vessel reviews.

 * **O/Due Office Response:** Displays vessels with overdue office responses.

 * **Rest Hours Conflict:** Displays vessels with rest hour conflicts.

 * <u>**Incomplete Data:** Displays vessels with incomplete operational data.</u>

screenshot: RH Dashboard - Office interface showing Performance Overview, Periodic Analysis, Rank Wise Violations, Vessel Status Overview, Vessel Analysis, and Rank Wise NCs. An orange callout box points to the Vessel Status Overview section with the text "Clicking on any graph element displays the corresponding records accordingly."

<a id="figure-86"></a>Figure 86

FULL ANSWER:
- **Green:** The crew member’s onboard service period is active or ongoing.
- **Yellow:** The crew member is approaching the relief due period.
- **Red:** The crew member is overdue for relief.

**Source:** Crewing User Manual R2_10.06.2026, section 1.5.1.1 “How to Access Crew Due / Overdue Status,” p. 39.

* The Vessel Status Overview section provides a summarized view of vessel operational and compliance status based on review, response, rest hours, and data completeness. (Ref Figure 86)

 * **O/Due Vessel Review:** Displays vessels with overdue vessel reviews.

 * **O/Due Office Response:** Displays vessels with overdue office responses.

 * **Rest Hours Conflict:** Displays vessels with rest hour conflicts.

 * <u>**Incomplete Data:** Displays vessels with incomplete operational data.</u>

<a id="figure-86"></a>Figure 86

FULL ANSWER:
On the Crew Due/Overdue timeline:

- **Green:** The crew member’s onboard service is active or ongoing.
- **Yellow:** The crew member is approaching the relief due period.
- **Red:** The crew member is overdue for relief.

Source: *Crewing User Manual R2_10.06.2026*, section **1.5.1.1 How to Access Crew Due / Overdue Status**, p. 39.

FULL ANSWER:
1. For a new defect report, clicking **Submit** saves the updated reporting details and forwards the report to the next stage of the configured defect-management workflow.

2. For a new **Condition Of Class (COC)** defect record, the same steps apply as **section 1.1.4.3, “How to Create a New Defect,” page 15**: clicking **Submit** saves the details and forwards the report to the next workflow stage.

**Source:**
- Standard new defect: *Defects User Manual for Office_Sail Admin*, §1.1.4.3, p.15.
- New COC defect: same steps as §1.1.4.3, p.15, per §1.1.5.2.

JUDGING RULE: must ['read-only', 'cannot modify']; must_not ['can be edited', 'editable by users']; expected Audit - Fleet Sharing Manual- Vessel_R1_30.06.2026.pdf p[5]. Evidence: The information displayed under Step 1 is shared by the Office for reference and is read-only. Vessel users cannot modify this information.
PER-RUN (current judges): E0 [True, True, False]  E1 [False, True, True]

==============================================================================================================
[E0] run 1 — verdict True — gate=answer cite=Audit - Fleet Sharing Manu p5 | 'No. Step 1 information is shared by the Office for reference and'
CITATIONS SHOWN TO THE USER:
  [1] Audit - Fleet Sharing Manual- Vessel_R1_30.06.2026 — Step 1 – Description (p.5)

  [4] Audit - Fleet Sharing Manual- Vessel_R1_30.06.2026 — 1.2 FLEET SHARING – OVERVIEW (p.4)

[1] (Audit - Fleet Sharing Manual- Vessel_R1_30.06.2026 — Step 1 – Description (p.5))
* <u>The information displayed under **Step 1** is shared by the Office for reference and is **read-only**.</u>

* <u>Vessel users cannot modify this information.</u>

---

[2] (Audit - Fleet Sharing Manual- Office_R1_30.06.2026 — 3.3 SUBMISSION (p.8))
This section allows users to update the recipient notification which is shared by the office.

* <u>Select the recipient(s) by All Vessels, Vessel, Fleet Group, Group, or Vessel Type.</u>
* <u>Select the issuer from the Issued By drop-down list.</u>
* <u>Click **Save** to save the submission details or **Submit** to complete and share the Fleet sharing with the</u> <u>selected recipient(s).</u>

<u>(See Figure 6)</u>

screenshot: Fleet Sharing software interface showing the Submission step with callouts for selecting recipients and clicking the submit button

Figure 6

[icon: hand pointing right] Users can edit the Fleet Sharing record until it has been submitted.
[icon: hand pointing right] Once submitted, no further changes can be made, and the information is shared with the selected vessel(s).

---

[3] (Audit - Fleet Sharing Manual- Vessel_R1_30.06.2026 — Step 2 – Discussion Record (p.5))
* <u>Enter the required Discussion Date under **Step 2**.</u>

* <u>Click **Submit** to confirm that the Fleet Sharing record has been discussed onboard. (See Figure 3)</u>

[icon: hand pointing right] Once the record is submitted, the **Confirmation** column in the Fleet Sharing overview table is automatically updated for both **Vessel** and **Office** users, indicating that the discussion has been completed.

---

[4] (Audit - Fleet Sharing Manual- Vessel_R1_30.06.2026 — 1.2 FLEET SHARING – OVERVIEW (p.4))
The **Fleet Sharing** sub-module provides a consolidated view of Fleet Sharing records received from Office. The information displayed in this sub-module is automatically synchronized with the **Vessel Fleet Sharing** form whenever updates are made.

* <u>Use the **Filters** option to refine the displayed records.</u>

* <u>Click **Apply** to filter the records or click **Clear** to reset the filters.</u>

* <u>Use the **Actions** column to view or edit Fleet Sharing records. (See Figure 2).</u>

---

[5] (Audit - Fleet Sharing Manual- Vessel_R1_30.06.2026 — Preamble (p.6))
screenshot: Fleet Sharing interface showing Description and Discussion Record sections with callouts explaining the UI elements.

<u>Figure 3</u>

FULL ANSWER:
No. Step 1 information is shared by the Office for reference and is read-only. Vessel users cannot modify it.

* <u>Use the **Filters** option to refine the displayed records.</u>

* <u>Click **Apply** to filter the records or click **Clear** to reset the filters.</u>

* <u>Use the **Actions** column to view or edit Fleet Sharing records. (See Figure 2).</u>

MANUALS — CASE hist-3
QUESTION: The manual explains how to record a negative observation with attachments — does that same process apply when I want to add a Positive Observation or LAE finding instead?
JUDGING RULE: must ['follow the above procedure', 'positive observations', 'lae']; must_not ['different procedure', 'not supported']; expected Audit - History Manual_R1_30.06.2026.pdf p[10]. Evidence: Follow the above procedure for adding Positive Observations and LAE finding details. Various types of inspection records can be created using the same procedure in SAIL.
PER-RUN (current judges): E0 [True, False, False]  E1 [False, False, False]

==============================================================================================================
[E0] run 1 — verdict True — gate=answer cite=Audit - History Manual_R1_ p10 | 'Yes. The manual states that the same procedure is used for **Pos'
CITATIONS SHOWN TO THE USER:
  [1] Audit - History Manual_R1_30.06.2026 — 2.1.4 ADDING ATTACHMENTS (p.10)
  [2] Audit - History Manual_R1_30.06.2026 — Document (p.13)
  [3] Audit - History Manual_R1_30.06.2026 — 2.1.2 OBSERVATION PAGE: (p.8)
  [4] Audit - Preparation Manual_Office_R1_30.06.2026 — 4.4.3 View Grounds for Negative Observations (p.23)
  [5] Audit - History Manual_R1_30.06.2026 — Preamble (p.9)

CAPTURED MODEL INPUT (the excerpts exactly as sent):
[1] (Audit - History Manual_R1_30.06.2026 — 2.1.4 ADDING ATTACHMENTS (p.10))
* <u>Click **Upload** to add the required observation attachments.</u>

* <u>Click **Save** (available at the top or bottom of the page) to save the finding details.</u>

* Click **Inspection** to return to the Inspection Records page. (See **Figure 10.**)

screenshot: Findings Details page with annotations pointing to Inspection, Upload, and Save buttons

Figure 10

☞ Follow the above procedure for adding Positive Observations and LAE finding details.

☞ Various types of inspection records can be created using the same procedure in SAIL.

---

[2] (Audit - History Manual_R1_30.06.2026 — Document (p.13))
* <u>The inspection and observation details are automatically imported, and an inspection record</u> <u>is created.</u>

* Click **Upload** to add supporting attachments manually, if required. (See **Figure 15.**)

screenshot_from_computer: SIRE 2.0 interface showing Inspection Details form with callouts: "The system automatically imports the Inspection and Observation details and creates the corresponding inspection record." and "Click on 'Upload' to add the attachments manually."

Figure 15

[icon: pointing hand] Observation details, including **Positive** and **LAE** findings, are automatically populated from the uploaded JSON file.

[icon: pointing hand] Additional supporting attachments, if any, must be uploaded manually.

---

[3] (Audit - History Manual_R1_30.06.2026 — 2.1.2 OBSERVATION PAGE: (p.8))
* <u>Select the desired **VIQ Ref.** The system will automatically populate the related fields,</u> <u>including **TMSA Ref**, **Initial Risk**, **Final Risk**, and **VIQ Question**.</u>

* <u>Add the **Ques Category**, **SOC**, and **NOC** related to the observation.</u>

* <u>Add a **Negative observation** in the text box.</u>

* <u>Click **Add New** to add the observation.</u>

* <u>Click the **Delete icon** to remove the current observation.</u>

---

[4] (Audit - Preparation Manual_Office_R1_30.06.2026 — 4.4.3 View Grounds for Negative Observations (p.23))
* <u>Click the **Grounds for -ve Obs** icon to view potential grounds for negative observations</u> <u>related to the question (See Figure 33).</u>

screenshot: SIRE 2.0 Questionnaire showing Potential Grounds for Neg Obs pop-up

Figure 33

---

[5] (Audit - History Manual_R1_30.06.2026 — Preamble (p.9))
screenshot: SIRE 2.0 Findings Details interface with annotations: Toggle between the Observation, Positive Finding & LAE page; Click "Save" to save the details; Select VIQ Ref from the dropdown; Delete current observation; Click here to "Add New" observation; Select Ques category, SOC, NOC from dropdown; Add "Negative Comments" here.

Figure 7

FULL ANSWER:
Yes. The manual states that the same procedure is used for **Positive Observations and LAE finding details**.

1. Select the **VIQ Ref.** The related fields—**TMSA Ref, Initial Risk, Final Risk, and VIQ Question**—are populated automatically.
2. Add the **Ques Category, SOC, and NOC**.
3. Enter the **Negative observation** in the text box when recording a negative observation.
4. Click **Add New** to add the observation. Use the **Delete** icon if needed.
5. Click **Upload** to add the required observation attachments.
6. Click **Save** at the top or bottom of the page.

For a **Positive Observation or LAE finding**, follow the same attachment and saving procedure; the manual explicitly says to follow the above procedure for those finding types.

There is also an import route: inspection and observation details, including Positive and LAE findings, can be populated automatically from an uploaded JSON file. Any additional supporting attachments must then be uploaded manually.

**Source:**  
- Manual entry and attachments: *Audit - History Manual_R1_30.06.2026*, sections **2.1.2 Observation Page** (p. 8), **Preamble** (p. 9), and **2.1.4 Adding Attachments** (p. 10).  
- JSON import and manual attachments: *Audit - History Manual_R1_30.06.2026*, **Document** (p. 13).

FULL ANSWER:

5. Click **Save** to save the finding details.

[5] (Incident - Incident User Manual R0_20.05.2026 — Part K: Office Closeout (p.18))
<mark>**!!** The **Office Closeout** section is intended for office users only. *(See Figure 20)*</mark>

screenshot: Part K - Office Closeout interface showing Reviewed checkbox, Office Comments text area, and Close button.

Figure 20

FULL ANSWER:
No. A vessel user must not fill in the closeout comments or close the incident report.

1. After all vessel actions are completed, the report remains open and requires closure by the office.
2. An office user reviews the report, enters **Office Comments**, marks **Reviewed**, and submits the closeout.
3. The **Office Closeout** section is for office users only; vessel users must not enter or modify information there.

Source: Office Closeout — *Incident User Manual*, Part K, p.18; *Near Miss User Manual*, Section 6, p.11. Pending Office Closure — *Incident User Manual*, p.19.

<mark>**!!** The **Office Closeout** section is intended for office users only. *(See Figure 20)*</mark>

screenshot: Part K - Office Closeout interface showing Reviewed checkbox, Office Comments text area, and Close button.

Figure 20

FULL ANSWER:
No. A vessel user must not enter or modify the Office Closeout section, including closeout comments. After vessel actions are complete, the incident remains open and requires closure by the office.

The office user completes the closeout review and comments, then closes the report.

Source:
- Incident User Manual R0_20.05.2026, “Part K: Office Closeout,” p. 18; “Pending Office Closure,” p. 19.
- Near Miss User Manual R1_30.05.2026, “6. Office Closeout,” p. 11.

MANUALS — CASE moc-office-1
QUESTION: In the MoC Log, what does clicking the eye icon actually do, and does that differ between the Office and Vessel versions of the manual?
JUDGING RULE: must ['view, edit, or export']; must_not ['delete the selected moc record', 'create a duplicate']; expected Safety - MOC User Manual_Office_R0_31.03.2026.pdf p[7]. Evidence: Office (p.7): eye icon = 'View Record' – 'View, edit, or export the selected MoC record.' Vessel (p.5): eye icon = 'View Attachments' – 'View the documents that have been attached to the MoC record.'

FULL ANSWER:
- **Office:** In **MoC Log**, select a record and click the **eye** icon to **view, edit, or export** the selected MoC record.
- **Vessel:** In **MoC Log**, select a record and click the **eye** icon to **view the documents attached** to that MoC record.

Yes, it differs: the Office manual describes the eye icon as a record view/edit/export action, while the Vessel manual describes it as viewing attachments.

**Source:** Office manual, “MoC Log – Overview,” p. 7; Vessel manual, “MoC Log – Overview,” p. 5.

MANUALS — CASE moc-office-2
QUESTION: When I click the Link icon in Part C's Risk Assessment section of an Office MoC, where does the saved RA actually end up?
JUDGING RULE: must ['ra sub-module', 'ra form']; must_not ['vessel risk assessment page']; expected Safety - MOC User Manual_Office_R0_31.03.2026.pdf p[13]. Evidence: Note: After selecting the relevant RA option, the user will be redirected to the RA form. Once the details are saved, the RA will be stored in the appropriate RA sub-module. Click the Back icon on the RA form to return to the MoC form.
PER-RUN (current judges): E0 [False, True, True]  E1 [True, True, True]

==============================================================================================================
[E0] run 1 — verdict False — gate=answer cite=Safety - Risk Assessment U p12 [expected page cited, not first] | 'The saved RA goes to the sub-module matching the option selected'
CITATIONS SHOWN TO THE USER:
  [1] Safety - Risk Assessment User Manual_Office_R1_30.05.2026 — 6.3 COMPLETING OFFICE RA - PART A (OVERVIEW) (p.12)
  [2] Safety - MOC User Manual_Office_R0_31.03.2026 — PART – C (Continued) (p.13)
  [3] Safety - Risk Assessment User Manual_Office_R1_30.05.2026 — 6.6 COMPLETING OFFICE RA - PART C (CHECKOUT) (p.16)
  [4] Safety - Risk Assessment User Manual_Office_R1_30.05.2026 — 6.1 HOW TO CREATE A OFFICE RISK ASSESSMENT (p.11)
  [5] Safety - Risk Assessment User Manual_Office_R1_30.05.2026 — 3. NAVIGATING THE RISK ASSESSMENT SUB-MODULE (p.6)

CAPTURED MODEL INPUT (the excerpts exactly as sent):
[1] (Safety - Risk Assessment User Manual_Office_R1_30.05.2026 — 6.3 COMPLETING OFFICE RA - PART A (OVERVIEW) (p.12))
I. Enter the required details under **A.1 Basic Information**.

II. Select the applicable **RA Type** using the available radio buttons.

III. Complete the task-related information under **A.2 Task Related Information** and **A.3 Alternative Method of Carrying Out the Task**, as applicable.

IV. Click **Save** to store the **Risk Assessment** details.
(See Figure 13)

screenshot: Risk Assessment Office software interface showing Part A - Overview with callouts for entering basic information, selecting RA type, entering task related information, and saving details.

Figure 13

☞ A **Report ID** is generated automatically once **Part–A** has been saved.

☞ **Part B** remains locked until Part A has been completed and saved.

---

[2] (Safety - MOC User Manual_Office_R0_31.03.2026 — PART – C (Continued) (p.13))
* To add any additional **Implication**, click on the **"+Add Implication"** icon.

* Proceed to **Section C3 – Risk Assessment**, where you can either upload an existing RA using the **Upload** icon or link a new RA using the **Link** icon.

* After clicking the Link icon, select either Office RA or Vessel RA as applicable.
 *(See Figure 12)*

☞ **Note:** Any additional implication can be deleted using the **Delete** icon.
All actions linked to that implication will also be deleted.
After deletion, review the **Part E – Action table** to ensure data consistency.

☞ **Note:** After selecting the relevant **RA option**, the user will be redirected to the **RA form**.
Once the details are saved, the RA will be stored in the appropriate **RA sub-module**.
Click the **Back** icon on the RA form to return to the MoC form.

screenshot: MoC form interface showing Section C3 Risk Assessment with callouts for adding implications and linking RAs

<u>Figure 12</u>

---

[3] (Safety - Risk Assessment User Manual_Office_R1_30.05.2026 — 6.6 COMPLETING OFFICE RA - PART C (CHECKOUT) (p.16))
* All actions added in *Part- B* are displayed under **"C.3 Action Status"**

I. Fill in all required details in **Part C** as applicable.

II. Use **Edit** icon to modify or edit a particular action or **Delete** icon to remove it.

III. Enter **RA Approval** information as per company requirements by selecting the **Level, Status, Approval Date**, and **Approver** details accordingly.

IV. Click **Submit** to save *Part C* information.

(See Figure 21)

screenshot: Risk Assessment Office software interface showing Part C Checkout section with callout boxes explaining the Action Status and Approval sections.

Figure 21

---

[4] (Safety - Risk Assessment User Manual_Office_R1_30.05.2026 — 6.1 HOW TO CREATE A OFFICE RISK ASSESSMENT (p.11))
* In the Office RA section, click on ‘**+ New RA**’ button to create new Office Risk Assessment. (See Figure 11)

screenshot: Risk Assessment interface showing the Office Risk Assessment section with a callout pointing to the + New RA button

<u>Figure 11</u>

☞ New Risk Assessment can be created in three ways: (See Figure 12)

* Import from the ‘**Generic Library.**’

* Import an existing Risk Assessment from the ‘**Office Library.**’

* Create a fresh Risk Assessment by clicking ‘**Create Fresh RA**’

screenshot: Office Risk Assessment list with a pop-up window titled New Risk Assessment showing three options: Generic Library, Office Library, and Create Fresh RA

<u>Figure 12</u>

---

[5] (Safety - Risk Assessment User Manual_Office_R1_30.05.2026 — 3. NAVIGATING THE RISK ASSESSMENT SUB-MODULE (p.6))
* The Risk Assessment sub-module is divided into the following sections, accessible from the left sidebar:

 * **Generic RA Library** – View and manage the generic Risk Assessment library.

 * **Office RA** – Create and manage office Risk Assessments.

 * **Vessels RA** – View and manage vessel-specific Risk Assessments.

 * **Key Personnel** – Manage personnel involved in the Risk Assessment approval process.

(See Figure 5)

screenshot: Risk Assessment sub-module interface showing Generic RA Library with a list of risk assessments and a sidebar for navigation.

<u>Figure 5</u>

<mark>icon: hand pointer The selected section in the sidebar is highlighted to indicate the active section.</mark>

FULL ANSWER:
The saved RA goes to the sub-module matching the option selected after clicking **Link**:

1. Click **Link** in Section C3.
2. Select **Office RA** or **Vessel RA**.
3. Complete and save the RA. It is stored in the corresponding **Office RA** or **Vessels RA** sub-module.
4. Click **Back** to return to the MoC form.

**Source:**  
- MoC User Manual, **Part C (Continued), p.13**  
- Risk Assessment User Manual, **3. Navigating the Risk Assessment Sub-Module, p.6**

   - **Office RA** → **Office RA** sub-module
   - **Vessel RA** → **Vessels RA** sub-module

You can then click **Back** on the RA form to return to the MoC form.

MANUALS — CASE pmsoffice-1
QUESTION: In the Work Orders section, what's the difference between a job showing 'Due' status versus 'Overdue' status?
JUDGING RULE: must ['current period', 'past their due date']; must_not ['cancelled', 'rejected']; expected Technical - PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026.pdf p[28]. Evidence: Due: Jobs that are due within the current period. Overdue: Jobs past their due date that have not been completed.
PER-RUN (current judges): E0 [False, True, True]  E1 [True, True, True]

==============================================================================================================
[E0] run 1 — verdict False — gate=answer cite=Technical - PMS User Manua p23 [expected page cited, not first] | '- **Due:** The job is due within the current period.\n- **Overdue'
CITATIONS SHOWN TO THE USER:
  [1] Technical - PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific_R3_08.07.2026 — 1.1.5.1 HOW TO ACCESS WORK ORDERS (W. O) (p.23)
  [2] Technical - PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — 1.1.5.1 HOW TO ACCESS WORK ORDERS (W. O) (p.28)
  [3] Technical - PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific_R3_08.07.2026 — 1.1.5.6 HOW TO COMPLETE THE WORK ORDER (p.27)
  [4] Technical - PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific_R3_08.07.2026 — TECHNICAL USER MANUAL (p.29)

CAPTURED MODEL INPUT (the excerpts exactly as sent):
[1] (Technical - PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific_R3_08.07.2026 — 1.1.5.1 HOW TO ACCESS WORK ORDERS (W. O) (p.23))
* Click the 'Work Orders' sub-module to view all the work orders. (Ref Figure 32)

* Click the required status tab (Scheduled, Due, Overdue, Postponed, Unplanned, Pending Approval, and Completed) to view the corresponding work orders' status. (Ref Figure 32)

screenshot: Work Orders (W\.O) interface showing navigation and status tabs with instructional callouts.

<a id="figure-32"></a>Figure 32

* [icon: hand pointing right] **Scheduled:** Jobs that have been planned and have a future due date.
* **Due:** Jobs that are due within the current period.
* **Overdue:** Jobs past their due date that have not been completed.
* **Postponed:** Jobs that have been deferred with an approved justification.
* **Unplanned:** Details of unplanned work orders will be displayed in this section.
* **Pending Approval:** All submitted work order details will be displayed here for HOD approval.
* **Completed:** Fully executed and approved 'Work Order' details will be displayed in this tab.

---

[2] (Technical - PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — 1.1.5.1 HOW TO ACCESS WORK ORDERS (W. O) (p.28))
* Click the ‘Work Orders’ sub-sub-module to view all the work orders. (Ref Figure 42)

* Select the vessel from the ‘Vessel’ dropdown. (Ref Figure 42)

* Click the required status tab (Scheduled, Due, Overdue, Postponed, Unplanned, Pending Approval, and Completed) to view the corresponding work orders' status. (Ref Figure 42)

Screenshot of the Work Orders (W\.O) interface showing navigation steps: 1. Click here to access the 'Work Orders', 2. Click here to select a vessel, 3. Select a status tab to display the corresponding 'Work Orders'. A red note points to a postponement icon.

Figure 42

* Hand icon **Scheduled**: Jobs that have been planned and have a future due date.
* **Due**: Jobs that are due within the current period.
* **Overdue**: Jobs past their due date that have not been completed.
* **Postponed**: Jobs that have been deferred with an approved justification.
* **Unplanned**: Details of unplanned work orders will be displayed in this section.
* **Pending Approval**: All submitted work order details will be displayed here for HOD approval.
* **Completed**: Fully executed and approved ‘Work Order’ details will be displayed in this tab.

---

[3] (Technical - PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific_R3_08.07.2026 — 1.1.5.6 HOW TO COMPLETE THE WORK ORDER (p.27))
* Go to the 'Work Orders' sub-sub module. (Ref Figure 39)

* Click the required status tab (Scheduled, Due, Overdue, Postponed, or Unplanned). (Ref Figure 39)

* Then select the work order by clicking the 'Edit' icon or the table row. (Ref Figure 39)

screenshot: Work Orders (W\.O) dashboard showing status tabs and work order list with instructional callouts

Figure 39

* The 'Work Order Form' appears after clicking the 'Edit' icon. (Ref Figure 40)

* Navigate to 'Part B – Work Completion Record', enter the required information. (Ref Figure 40)

screenshot: Part B - Work Completion Record form showing risk assessment and work duration fields with instructional callouts

Figure 40

☞ To upload RA documents, ensure the radio button is not set to NA or No.

---

[4] (Technical - PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific_R3_08.07.2026 — TECHNICAL USER MANUAL (p.29))
* After entering all the required information, click 'Save Draft' to save the record as a draft or click the 'Save' button to submit for approval. (Ref Figure 43)

screenshot: Work Completion Record form showing 'Save Draft' and 'Submit' buttons with a callout 'Click here to save and submit the record for approval.'

Figure 43

* Click the 'Overdue' tab from the header. (Ref Figure 44)

* Click on the '+ Set Reason' button which is in the status column in the table. (Ref Figure 44)

screenshot: Work Orders (W\.O) dashboard with callouts: '1. Click on 'Overdue' tab.' and '2. Click here to set a reason for Overdue WO.'

Figure 44

* The screen below appears after clicking the '+ Set Reason' button. (Ref Figure 45)

* Select an 'Overdue Reason' from the dropdown, enter additional remarks or details if required, and then click the 'Save' button to save the record. (Ref Figure 45)

screenshot: Record Overdue Reason pop-up window with callouts: '1. Choose the 'Overdue Reason' from the dropdown.' and '2. Click here to save the record.'

Figure 45

FULL ANSWER:
- **Due:** The job is due within the current period.
- **Overdue:** The job has passed its due date and has not been completed. For an overdue work order, you can select **+ Set Reason**, choose an **Overdue Reason**, add remarks if required, and click **Save**.

**Source:** Technical PMS User Manual—Vessel Specific, §1.1.5.1 “How to Access Work Orders,” p.23; §1.1.5.6 “How to Complete the Work Order,” pp.29.

Figure 43

* Click the 'Overdue' tab from the header. (Ref Figure 44)

* Click on the '+ Set Reason' button which is in the status column in the table. (Ref Figure 44)

screenshot: Work Orders (W\.O) dashboard with callouts: '1. Click on 'Overdue' tab.' and '2. Click here to set a reason for Overdue WO.'

Figure 44

* The screen below appears after clicking the '+ Set Reason' button. (Ref Figure 45)

* Select an 'Overdue Reason' from the dropdown, enter additional remarks or details if required, and then click the 'Save' button to save the record. (Ref Figure 45)

screenshot: Record Overdue Reason pop-up window with callouts: '1. Choose the 'Overdue Reason' from the dropdown.' and '2. Click here to save the record.'

Figure 45

FULL ANSWER:
- **Due:** Jobs that are due within the current period.
- **Overdue:** Jobs past their due date that have not been completed.

For an overdue work order, the manual also provides a **“+ Set Reason”** action: select an overdue reason, add remarks if needed, and save it.

Source: Vessel PMS User Manual, section 1.1.5.1 “How to Access Work Orders” (p. 23); Office Sail Admin PMS User Manual, section 1.1.5.1 (p. 28); Vessel PMS User Manual, “Technical User Manual” (pp. 29).

Figure 90

Page 54 of 64

FULL ANSWER:
No. As a **Vessel User**, the Modify PMS change-request option is not available. The Modify PMS screen is displayed only to the **HOD**, so you cannot create a new request yourself.

Source: Technical - PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific_R3_08.07.2026, section 1.1.10.1 “How to Access Modify PMS,” p. 47.

QUESTION: If I change the assigned inspector on an existing preparation record, which fields can't be changed, and what happens to the vessel's answers that were already entered?
JUDGING RULE: must ['viq version', 'viq group', 'permanently removes']; must_not ['responses are retained', 'no impact']; expected Audit - Preparation Manual_Office_R1_30.06.2026.pdf p[11]. Evidence: All fields can be edited except VIQ Version and VIQ Group. Important: Changing the Inspector permanently removes all vessel responses associated with the previously assigned Inspector for that preparation.

  [1] Audit - Preparation Manual_Office_R1_30.06.2026 — 2.7 EDITING EXISTING PREPARATION (p.11)

[1] (Audit - Preparation Manual_Office_R1_30.06.2026 — 2.7 EDITING EXISTING PREPARATION (p.11))
* <u>Click the **Edit** icon to make changes to the initial information of the existing preparation.</u>

<u>(See Figure 13)</u>

screenshot: Preparation dashboard showing a list of vessels with an arrow pointing to the Edit icon

Figure 13

<mark>Click Edit icon</mark>

* <u>A pop-up window will appear, modify the required preparation details. All fields can be</u> <u>edited **except VIQ Version** and **VIQ Group**. (See Figure 14)</u>

* <u>If the **Inspector** has been changed, click **Confirm** in the confirmation message to proceed.</u>

* <u>Click **Save Changes** to update the preparation record.</u>

[icon: hand pointing right] If the Inspector is changed, an email notification is automatically sent to both the Vessel and the Office informing them of the change.

[icon: hand pointing right] The email recipients for **Inspector Change Notifications** are configurable and can be set as per the client's notification requirements.

[icon: hand pointing right] This functionality is **domain/client configurable**.

**!! <u>Important:** Changing the **Inspector** permanently removes all vessel responses associated with the</u> previously assigned Inspector for that preparation.

---

[2] (Audit - Preparation Manual_Office_R1_30.06.2026 — Preamble (p.12))
screenshot: Vessel inspection preparation form with a confirmation dialog for changing the inspector. A yellow callout points to the "Save Changes" button.

Figure 14

---

[3] (Audit - Preparation Manual_Office_R1_30.06.2026 — 3.1 HOW TO CREATE NEW PREPARATION (p.13))
* Click '**+ New Preparation**' to create a new preparation record.

* <u>A **Preparation Details** window is displayed. Select the required **Inspection Type**.</u>

* <u>Complete all mandatory preparation details by selecting the appropriate values from</u> <u>the drop-down lists and entering the required information.</u>

* <u>Click **Save Changes** to create the preparation record or **Discard** to cancel without saving.</u>

* <u>The newly created preparation record is added to the Preparation listing and is ready for</u> <u>further actions.</u>

(See Figure 18)

screenshot_from_computer: Software interface showing the creation of a new preparation record with callouts explaining the steps.

Figure 18

<mark>☞ An email notification is automatically sent to the **Vessel** when a new preparation record is created.</mark>

---

[4] (Audit - History Manual_R1_30.06.2026 — 2.1 METHOD 1: MANUAL DATA ENTRY (p.7))
* Click **+ New Record** to add an inspection record. (See **Figure 4.**)

screenshot: SIRE 2.0 History page showing the + New Record button

Figure 4

* <u>Select the **Vessel** from the dropdown menu.</u>

* <u>Select the desired **Inspection Type** from the dropdown menu.</u>

* Click **Add New** to create the inspection record. (See **Figure 5.**)

screenshot: Manual Data Entry modal with Vessel and Inspection Type dropdowns and Add New button

Figure 5

---

[5] (Audit - Preparation Manual_Office_R1_30.06.2026 — 2.4 PIQ ICON (p.8))
* <u>Click the **PIQ** button corresponding to a preparation record to open the **Pre-Inspection</u> Questionnaire (PIQ)**.

* <u>The percentage displayed on the **PIQ** button indicates the current completion progress of</u> <u>the questionnaire.</u>

(See Figure 8)

screenshot: Table showing vessel inspection details with a highlighted PIQ button showing 99% progress.

Figure 8

☞ A **Pre-Inspection Questionnaire (PIQ)** is created automatically with every new preparation.

☞ The **PIQ responses from the previous Preparation** are automatically copied to the new PIQ. Users can review and modify the copied responses as required.

☞ The completion percentage is updated automatically as the PIQ is completed.

FULL ANSWER:
On an existing preparation record:

- **VIQ Version** and **VIQ Group** cannot be changed.
- Changing the **Inspector** permanently removes all vessel responses associated with the previously assigned Inspector for that preparation.
- You must click **Confirm** in the confirmation message, then **Save Changes**.

Source: *Audit - Preparation Manual_Office_R1_30.06.2026*, §2.7 “Editing Existing Preparation,” p.11.

2. You can edit all fields **except VIQ Version and VIQ Group**.
3. If you change the **Inspector**, click **Confirm** in the confirmation message, then click **Save Changes**.
4. All vessel responses associated with the previously assigned Inspector are **permanently removed**.

MANUALS — CASE ra-office-3
QUESTION: Do the filters work the same way in the Generic RA Library, Office RA, and Vessels RA sections, or is each explained separately?
JUDGING RULE: must ['same across', 'office ra']; must_not ['different filter fields for each section']; expected Safety - Risk Assessment User Manual_Office_R1_30.05.2026.pdf p[7]. Evidence: The filtering functionality is same across the Generic RA Library, Office RA, and Vessels RA sections. The available filter options vary depending on the selected section. The example below demonstrates the filtering process in the Office RA section.
PER-RUN (current judges): E0 [True, False, True]  E1 [True, False, False]

  [2] Safety - Risk Assessment User Manual_Vessel_R1_30.05.2026 — 3. HOW TO APPLY FILTERS (p.6)
  [3] Safety - Risk Assessment User Manual_Vessel_R1_30.05.2026 — 4. HOW TO VIEW GENERIC RISK ASSESSMENT REPORT (p.7)

CAPTURED MODEL INPUT (the excerpts exactly as sent):
[1] (Safety - Risk Assessment User Manual_Office_R1_30.05.2026 — 4. HOW TO APPLY FILTERS (p.7))
☞ The filtering functionality is same across the **Generic RA Library**, **Office RA**, and **Vessels RA** sections.

☞ The available filter options vary depending on the selected section.

☞ The example below demonstrates the filtering process in the Office RA section.

* In the Office RA section, click the **Filters** button to display the available filtering options.

* Select the required filter options:

 * **Period** – Filter records by time-period.

 * **Department** – Display Risk Assessments for a specific department.

 * **RA Type** – Filter records by the selected RA type.

 * **Search** – Enter keywords to search by Report ID or RA Name.

* Click **Apply** to display the filtered results.

* Click **Clear** to remove all selected filters and display the complete list of Risk Assessments.

(See Figure 6)

screenshot: Risk Assessment software interface showing the Office RA section with filter options and a list of risk assessments. Annotations point to the Filters button, the filter selection area, and the Apply/Clear buttons.

Figure 6

---

[2] (Safety - Risk Assessment User Manual_Vessel_R1_30.05.2026 — 3. HOW TO APPLY FILTERS (p.6))
* Click on the **‘Filters’** tab to see filtering options.

* Select the required filters to view reports based on the desired **time period, group, sub-group, vessel type,** and **RA type.**

* Click **‘Apply’** to filter the results or **‘Clear’** to reset the selection.
 (See Figure 5)

screenshot_from_computer: Risk Assessment software interface showing filter options with callouts: 1. Click on Filter tab to see filtering options, 2. Select desired Filter to view as required, 3. Click to Apply or clear the filter selection.

<u>Figure 5</u>

☞ The filter functionality is the same for both Generic Risk Assessment (GRA) and Vessel Risk Assessment (VRA).

---

[3] (Safety - Risk Assessment User Manual_Vessel_R1_30.05.2026 — 4. HOW TO VIEW GENERIC RISK ASSESSMENT REPORT (p.7))
* The **Generic RA Library** opens by default when the RA sub-module is accessed.

* From sidebar menu, users can switch between either **‘Generic RA Library’** or **‘Vessel RA’** based on their requirements.

*(See Figure 6)*

screenshot: Generic Risk Assessment library interface showing the sidebar menu with Generic RA Library and Vessels RA options highlighted

Figure 6

* Click on the **‘Eye’** icon to view the ‘Risk Assessment’ form. *(See Figure 7)*

screenshot: Risk Assessment table showing an eye icon highlighted with a callout 'Click to view GRA form'

Figure 7

icon: hand pointing right The **GRA Library** is available to vessel users for **viewing purposes only**.

FULL ANSWER:
The filtering process is the same across the **Generic RA Library, Office RA, and Vessels RA** sections, but the available filter options vary by section.

1. **Generic RA Library**
   - Open **Filters**.
   - Select the required options.
   - Click **Apply** to show the filtered results, or **Clear** to reset them.
   - Vessel users have viewing access to the Generic RA Library only.

2. **Office RA**
   - Click **Filters**.
   - Select **Period**, **Department**, **RA Type**, or enter keywords in **Search** by Report ID or RA Name.
   - Click **Apply**, or click **Clear** to show the complete list.

3. **Vessel RA**
   - Click the **Filters** tab.
   - Select the required **time period, group, sub-group, vessel type,** or **RA type**.
   - Click **Apply**, or click **Clear** to reset the selection.

**Source:**  
- Generic RA Library, Office RA, and Vessels RA: *Safety - Risk Assessment User Manual_Office_R1_30.05.2026*, §4, p.7.  
- Vessel RA filtering: *Safety - Risk Assessment User Manual_Vessel_R1_30.05.2026*, §3, p.6.  
- Generic RA Library access: *Safety - Risk Assessment User Manual_Vessel_R1_30.05.2026*, §4, p.7.

FULL ANSWER:
The filtering process works the same way across all three sections; it is not a separate procedure for each:

1. Open **Filters** (or the **Filters** tab).
2. Select the required options.
   - **Office RA:** Period, Department, RA Type, or Search by Report ID/RA Name.
   - **Generic RA Library/Vessels RA:** time period, group, sub-group, vessel type, and RA type.
3. Click **Apply** to display the filtered results.
4. Click **Clear** to reset the filters and show the complete list.

The available filter options vary by section. The Vessel manual specifically states that filtering is the same for the **Generic Risk Assessment (GRA)** and **Vessel Risk Assessment (VRA)** sections.

**Source:**  
- *Safety - Risk Assessment User Manual_Office_R1_30.05.2026*, §4 “How to Apply Filters,” p.7.  
- *Safety - Risk Assessment User Manual_Vessel_R1_30.05.2026*, §3 “How to Apply Filters,” p.6.

screenshot: Risk Assessment table showing an eye icon highlighted with a callout 'Click to view GRA form'

Figure 7

icon: hand pointing right The **GRA Library** is available to vessel users for **viewing purposes only**.

FULL ANSWER:
The filtering process works the same way across the **Generic RA Library, Office RA, and Vessels RA** sections, but the available filter options vary by section. They are not separate procedures.

1. **Office RA**
   1. Click **Filters**.
   2. Select the required **Period**, **Department**, **RA Type**, or **Search** options. Search uses Report ID or RA Name.
   3. Click **Apply** to show the filtered results.
   4. Click **Clear** to remove the filters and show all Risk Assessments.

2. **Generic RA Library and Vessels RA**
   1. Click the **Filters** tab.
   2. Select filters for **time period, group, sub-group, vessel type,** and **RA type**, as required.
   3. Click **Apply** to filter the results, or **Clear** to reset the selection.

The Vessel manual explicitly states that filtering is the same for **Generic Risk Assessment (GRA)** and **Vessel Risk Assessment (VRA)**. The Generic RA Library opens by default, and vessel users have viewing-only access.

**Source:**  
- Office filtering: *Safety - Risk Assessment User Manual_Office_R1_30.05.2026*, Section 4, p. 7.  
- Generic/Vessel filtering: *Safety - Risk Assessment User Manual_Vessel_R1_30.05.2026*, Section 3, p. 6.  
- Generic RA Library access: *Safety - Risk Assessment User Manual_Vessel_R1_30.05.2026*, Section 4, p. 7.

FULL ANSWER:
As a vessel user, you can only view Generic Risk Assessments in the Generic RA Library; you cannot edit them there.

1. Open the **RA** sub-module. The **Generic RA Library** opens by default.
2. If needed, select **Generic RA Library** from the sidebar.
3. Click the **Eye** icon to open the Risk Assessment form in view-only mode.

The manual states that the GRA Library is available to vessel users for **viewing purposes only**.

Source: Safety - Risk Assessment User Manual_Vessel_R1_30.05.2026, section 4, “How to View Generic Risk Assessment Report,” p. 7.

FULL ANSWER:
As a vessel user, you can **only view** Generic Risk Assessments in the Generic RA Library; you cannot edit them there.

1. Open the **Generic RA Library**.
2. Click the **Eye** icon to open the Risk Assessment form.
3. The library is **view-only for vessel users**.

To edit an assessment, use the **Vessel RA** section after importing it into the Vessel Library or creating a Vessel RA.

Source: Vessel Risk Assessment User Manual, “How to View Generic Risk Assessment Report,” p.7; “How to Create New Vessel Risk Assessment,” p.10.
